## 1. What a call takes, by some nine words

Agreement 2/3, size M.

**Road.** The maintainer's road: modifier for what a step writes after its verb, args for the values its call computes, input for what a function declares; `params` becomes `inputs`; object, argument, param and part leave the language; standard input takes a section of its own name.


**Decision.** A command is a verb and its modifiers: a modifier is what a step writes after its verb, held as the step it is under `:modifiers` of `::call`, `~(add x) | first | get /modifiers` answering `[::call{:name :x}]`. The args are the values the modifiers computed against the subject, one value under the tag of the verb, `::number/add[1 2]`, an element of them an arg: a recipe reads them as `args`, `apply` and `fill` take them, `5 | apply f [3]`, a call that gives more than its inputs read is refused as `…/ArgsBeyondInputsError`, and a fold calls its verb with the value so far as the subject and the element as its first arg. An input is what a function declares by `takes`: its name, its page, its place, a path or code over the args, `/0` or `~(query /0 1)`, the arg it reads by its index, and its kinds; `inputs` answers them, `::fn | takes :by |~~ b ~~| /0 ::number | impl ~(add by) | inputs | first | keys` answering `#[:kinds :name :page :place]`. The flags of `toRegex` are its input `flags`, `toRegex "i"`. A query starts from its subject, the value it is given, which the command line reads from standard input [QSHST#stdin]; object names a JSON object alone and argument an argument of the command line's process alone; the language server says inputs where it shows a verb's, the fields its protocol names staying the protocol's.


**Why.** The maintainer's answer binds, and [D351] records it as the direction of the vocabulary. One fact, one spelling: the tree tells three things apart, the step written, the value computed and the role declared, and [QSBLD#input] already defines modifier, args and input apart. Words carry paradigms and [D349]: args stands beside `args` in nearly all its matches, object is among the commonest words of other projects and wakes the receiver of a call and the object of JSON at once, and param beside input would name one role twice. Input keeps one sense once standard input takes its own section and the first page stops calling the data of a query an input.


**Rationale.** Deciders 1 and 2 took the maintainer's road; decider 3 kept `params` beside `inputs` as the habit's road of [QSWRK#catalog], which his answer sets aside (param leaves) and which no task asks for, so it does not outweigh his choice. Where the two parted in detail: the flags of `toRegex` are its input `flags` (deciders 2 and 3; `::string/toRegex` declares `:flags`, its page at `::string/toRegex/flags`, place `~(query /0 null)`); the arg an input reads is named by its index (decider 2, agreeing with part 14); the fold's element is its first arg, the word the pages of `reduce` already write (`::vec/reduce | doc`); and standard input takes the section [QSHST#stdin] (decider 1), so input keeps the one sense the maintainer gave it, stdin being the module's own name, `cli/src/io-stdin.mjs`. Measured: input stands in the files naming `takes` in most of its matches, args beside `args` in nearly all, object in thousands of files of the outside corpus.


**Cost.** `::fn/params` renamed `::fn/inputs` with its refusal `::fn/params/SubjectNotFnError`, its laws and the case `params-answers-the-inputs-of-a-function` of QSBLD/fn. The pages of `::fn` (object, arguments, params, the laws that say object or arguments, the page of `fill`), `::kind`, `apply` in `core/lib/qlang/any.qlang` and the pages of `reduce` where they say object; the first page (“the input it is given”, “the element as the modifier”, “takes no object”, “each of its objects”, the laws `:takesNoObjectForARecipeWrittenAlone`, `:looksAtTheInput` and `:looksAtTheShapeOfTheInput` renamed by their rule); the repair of `toRegex` (`REPAIRS` in `core/src/pattern.mjs`) with its law, renamed with part 23; the head of `cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang` (“the parts”), `site/src/pages/index.astro`, `core/README.md` (“the parts of its args”). Sections at the move: [QSBLD#fn] and #input (“a key of an object”), [QSQUO#held] (“an object of a call”), [QSCOL#reduce], [QSNUM#arithmetic] and #combine, [QSSLF#catalog], and [QSHST#input] renamed [QSHST#stdin], its text saying standard input, with the citations of `cli/src`, `cli/test`, `core/src/walk.mjs`, `core/test/unit/walk.test.mjs`, `scripts/sensors/run-probes.mjs`, [D257] and [D340]. Code: `objectsOf` of `core/src/runtime/signature.mjs` named `modifiersOf`, `codeOfObject` and `refusalOfFailedObject` of `core/src/eval.mjs` named for the modifier, `selectorOfPlace` and `.selector` of `core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs` named for the place and its number `.place` an `.index`. The probes that call `params`, this record's and those of [D339] parts 3 and 8, hold the new answer. `params` enters the lexicon [D350]. A query or the sister project that calls `params` breaks in one release.


**Removes.** Flagged: the verb name `params` (`::fn/params`), renamed `inputs` on the maintainer's answer; the section anchor [QSHST#input], renamed [QSHST#stdin]. No capability, form or shape of a value.


**The maintainer's words.** «modifier / input / args (Рекомендую)» (maintainer, 2026-10-16 12:21 UTC, session d7f52e95), the option put to him as a step writing modifiers, a function declaring inputs (params→inputs), the value of a call its args, and object, argument, param and part leaving; «ок, давай args» (2026-10-09 18:03, 22b934f8), after «svoObject тогда как-то наверное лучше .. что б усилить сигнал.. и не мешать с ооп.. такое допустимо? ..» (18:02); «::Box/put[10]  итого это у нас раскладывается на  ::Box - S (subject), put - V (verb), а vec [10] - это O (object)» (2026-10-09 03:33, 22b934f8); «а то что у нас в проекте не было и даже порицилось использование всякой классической объектно-ориентированной замусоренной терминологии в виде класса и объекта и т.п..» (2026-09-23 08:28, 86982eb5).


**Files.** In D:/git/qlang: core/lib/qlang.qlang, core/lib/qlang/fn.qlang, kind.qlang, any.qlang, vec.qlang, set.qlang, map.qlang, regex.qlang, string.qlang, cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang, core/src/runtime/signature.mjs, core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs, core/src/eval.mjs, core/src/pattern.mjs, core/src/walk.mjs, cli/src/main.mjs, cli/src/script-mode.mjs, cli/src/io-stdin.mjs, lsp/src/features.mjs, site/src/pages/index.astro, core/README.md, docs/spec/QSBLD-functions-and-kinds.md, QSQUO-code-as-data.md, QSCOL-collections.md, QSNUM-numbers.md, QSSLF-the-language-describes-itself.md, QSHST-hosts-and-the-command-line.md, core/test/conformance/QSBLD/fn.jsonl, docs/journal/D276.md, D272.md, D257.md, D339.md, D340.md


**Checker: refuted.** The road itself holds. [QSBLD#input] already tells modifiers, args and inputs apart, `args` stands 99% beside `args` and `input` 82% in the files naming `takes`. The decision as written fails on six counts. ## 2. The role of a tag that names what lies under it: label, noun, kind, tag and topic

Agreement 3/3, size S.

**Road.** The defined terms kept, each use repaired to its term: one definition of a label, written in [QSNAM#address] and cited by [QSBLD#label]; noun the head of any address; topic the label whose page tells the language or a host, no third kind of name.


**Decision.** A tag names a kind and is a value of its own, `::Box`; a kind is what a value is, the tag `type` answers, a tag no declaration answers naming a kind of identity. A label is a kind declared under a name, by the chain that builds it from `::kind` or by its page alone, which declares a kind of no commands: its verbs reside on it, its nodes lie under its address, `kinds` answers it, and its tag lays itself over a payload through its constructor, `:Box |~~ B ~~| | ::Box | get [~(binding | get /value | type) ~(kinds | has ::Box) ~(::Box[1] | type)]` answering `[::doc true ::Box]` [QSNAM#address]; [QSBLD#label] cites that definition and says what a kind declared by a chain adds. A noun is the name an address names before its last name, declared before the names under it, else `::bind/NounNotDeclaredError`, whatever it holds: a label, `::Box` of `::Box/area`, a verb, `::number/add` of `::number/add/addend`, or a name of data, `:x` of `:x/y`. The topic of the language is the label `::qlang`, whose page is the first page, and the topic of the command line the label `::cli`; what `tells` declares under a topic is a chapter, the page of a concept, and no kind. A label, a topic among them, is reached by its tag, `::string`, `::qlang`.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: the two definitions of a label become one once a label of a page alone is the kind of no commands the first page already calls it. The order of concepts is the order of dependencies: [QSNAM] stands before [QSBLD] (`docs/spec/README.md`), and [QSNAM#address] holds the label among the nodes of the tree of names before any chain builds one. Words carry paradigms: label (ярлык) and noun are the maintainer's words, each of one sense the tree keeps, and folding them into kind and tag would leave the noun of a verb or of data with no term, the critique's counterexample.


**Rationale.** All three keep the defined terms and write a label once. Deciders 1 and 2 write the definition in [QSNAM#address], decider 3 in [QSBLD#label]; [QSNAM#address] holds it by the order of the chapters and because a label is a node of the tree of names before it is a kind a chain builds, [QSBLD#label] resting on it. The topic stays a label whose page tells, as all three read it. The probe of a label of a page alone answers `[::doc true ::Box]` on the tree.


**Cost.** No case, refusal or word of the surface. Sentences at the move: [QSNAM#address] (the one definition), [QSNAM#reach] (“A label, a kind or a topic, is reached by its tag” said of a label), the head of [QSBLD] (“a label is a kind”), [QSBLD#label], [QSSLF#page] (“its members are its chapters, the concepts”). The first page's chapter of verbs (“A kind is a label built from `::kind`…” and the label of a page alone, one sentence); the page of `kinds` in `core/lib/qlang/any.qlang` (“A concept of the topic is no kind” said of a chapter, its law `:holdsNoConcept` named for the chapter); the comments of `core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs` that list a topic beside a kind; the index of the site (“A kind is a label declared by its page”).


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «да, допиши потому что это важно .. и добавь в наш словарь терминов "легенда", "легенда полезной нагрузки" "ярлык"» (maintainer, 2026-10-08 23:30, session 22b934f8); «одно я знаю что надо держаться за существительные» (2026-10-07 17:29, 22b934f8); «существительное в начале сильнее снимает энтропию» (2026-10-08 00:44, 22b934f8).


**Files.** In D:/git/qlang: docs/spec/QSNAM-names-and-scope.md, QSBLD-functions-and-kinds.md, QSSLF-the-language-describes-itself.md, core/lib/qlang.qlang, core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, core/lib/qlang/kind.qlang, core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs, site/src/pages/index.astro


**Checker: refuted.** The road holds on the tree. A label declared by its page alone answers `[::doc true ::Box]`, a noun of data `:x/y` declares, and `kinds` lists `::qlang` and `::cli`. But the one definition it writes into [QSNAM#address], a label is a kind declared under a name, contradicts [QSNAM#root], which no part rewrites: «a label declared under a noun, `::qlang/values`, is read by `manifest` of its noun» (docs/spec/QSNAM-names-and-scope.md:277). `::qlang/values` holds a set, `kinds` leaves it out, and the decision itself calls it a chapter and no kind. After the move, one chapter would call a chapter a label while another says a label is a kind.

**Probe.** qlang 'get [~(kinds | has ::qlang/values) ~(::qlang/values | binding | get /value | type)]' → [false ::set]; qlang ':Box |~~ B ~~| | ::Box | get [~(binding | get /value | type) ~(kinds | has ::Box) ~(::Box[1] | type)]' → [::doc true ::Box]; git grep -n 'a label declared under a noun' -- docs/spec → QSNAM-names-and-scope.md:277 alone

**Repair.** Add [QSNAM#root] to the sentences at the move: «a chapter declared under a topic, `::qlang/values`, is read by `manifest` of its noun», and `kinds` answers the labels.


## 3. Verb and word

Agreement 3/3, size L.

**Road.** The maintainer's road: verb everywhere, the V of the subject–verb–object frame; word keeps its plain sense and is no term of the language.


**Decision.** A verb is a function named under a label, the label of the values it serves, which a command calls by its name, `::vec/sort` the `sort` of vectors; the walk of the subject's tags finds it [QSDSP#walk], and the chapter `::qlang/verbs` tells the verbs that live on kinds. The chapters, the first page, the pages, the refusals, the facts, the laws, the cases, the code and the tools say verb: the first page writes “A verb is a function named under a label”, the chapter QSDSP is “A verb found through its subject”, the section [QSNAM#word] is [QSNAM#verb], and a value the scope binds meets a verb of its name aloud, `get [~(5 | count !| type) ~(:count 5 | [1 2] | count !| type)]` answering `[::call/KindAnswersNoVerbError ::call/ValueMeetsVerbError]`. The core exports `verbNames`, `verbsReaching`, `isVerb` and `declaresVerb`; the hosts hand `tokenize` the verbs of the root as `verbNames`, and it paints the name of a call that reaches one as a `verb`. Word is no term of the language: it keeps its plain sense, a word of a text, `contains` finding a word of a string and a modifier of more than one word standing in parentheses, and `true`, `false` and `null` are the words of the literals.


**Why.** The maintainer's answer binds, and [D349] chose it: verb has one exact sense and the prior of subject, verb and object, where word names a function under a label, a word of a text, a token of a source and the name of a literal at once, the critique's counterexample, and a search for it brings a heap of senses. The surface already says verb, `::call/KindAnswersNoVerbError`, `verbNames`, `verbsReaching`, `::qlang/verbs`, the law keys of that chapter and the tasks of the benchmark, so this road renames one refusal, two exports and the texts. The option `builtinNames` called every verb of the root a builtin, a second sense of `::builtin`, which the maintainer holds up for its one domain.


**Rationale.** All three and the maintainer chose verb. Where they parted in detail: the painter's token and its option, which deciders 1 and 2 name `verb` and `verbNames` and decider 3 leaves to [D340] part 3, take here the word of the thing a call reaches, the rest of the vocabulary of `tokenize` staying D340's; the section [QSNAM#word] becomes [QSNAM#verb] (decider 3), its heading naming the thing it declares. No argument against his choice: the measure answers word in several times the matches of verb in our texts and in eight times the files of the outside corpus.


**Cost.** `::call/ValueMeetsWordError` renamed `::call/ValueMeetsVerbError` with its page, its laws and its cases (QSNAM/reach, QSCOL/reduce and the unit tests), and the open records that cite it, [D304] and [D326]; the law keys and the names of the cases that say word for a verb (`:isAWordNamedUnderALabel`, `:names/meetsAWordAloud`, `:readsThePageOfAWord`, `:quotesOneWord`, `:verbs/refusesTheTagForAWordOfValues` and their kin, `git grep -h -o -E "proves :[A-Za-z/]*Word[A-Za-z/]*" -- core/lib cli/lib`), each case under the record in transit over its section; every chapter of the language where word names a function under a label, the title and the file of `docs/spec/QSDSP-a-word-found-through-its-subject.md` with every link to it, the section [QSNAM#word] with its citations, the heads of [QSTXT] and [QSCOL], [QSWRK#catalog] (“A word enters the catalog”) and [QSHST#repl] (a `word`), `docs/spec/README.md`; the first page and its link text, every page of the catalog and `cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang`; the exports `isWord` and `declaresWord` of `core/src/index.mjs`, read by the language server and `cli/test/error-tag-catalog-drift.test.mjs`; the token `'word'` of `core/src/highlight.mjs` and its option `builtinNames` with the painters of the command line, the language server, the site and the editor; the names of the core (`applyWord`, `nearestWords`, `declaresWordIn`); the seeds (`CLAUDE.md`, the READMEs) and the aim where word means a verb. The completion kind the language server hands the editor stays the protocol's. The retired names enter the lexicon [D350]; word, a generic word, stays out of it. `node scripts/benchmark.mjs check` runs before the commit that rewrites the first page. A host whose pages say word, or that catches the refusal or imports `isWord`, renames in one release.

## 4. Member and node

Agreement 3/3, size M.

**Road.** Node for a place of the tree of names, a verb's value named by the verb, anchor for the name an axis reads; member and membership leave; the parser's node leaves with the evaluator over the steps.


**Decision.** The names form one tree, every node a binding: a node is what lies under a noun at an address, `::vec/sort` under `::vec`, and `manifest` answers the nodes under a noun. An axis given an anchor, a keyword, reads the node the subject holds under it, an input of a verb or the verb a name calls on a noun, `::map | doc :minus` the page of `::map/minus`; a vector of anchors is a path of nodes; the chapters of a topic are its nodes, and the names near a miss are the nodes near it. The value of a verb, a function or a recipe under a label, is what runs on the values of its label (`isVerbValue`, `callVerbOn`). Node names nothing else in the language: the parser reads a source into the records of its steps and their spans, its tree leaving with the evaluator over the steps [D339]; `:node` is the proper name of the platform Node; and a vector or a set holds an element or lacks it, no membership said.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms: member is a word of classes and objects, which the maintainer asked the project to keep away from, and the sister project's domain names the members of Java classes with it. One fact, one spelling: node is the first page's word (“a node under the noun”) and that of [QSNAM#address] (“every node a binding”), and the parser's tree, its other sense, leaves with the maintainer's one model of code [D339] part 1, so node keeps one sense in the language.


**Rationale.** All three chose node and let member go. Decider 1 alone saw that the maintainer's one model of code, [D339] part 1 («Одна модель — шаги (Рекомендую)», 2026-10-16 12:22 UTC, session d7f52e95), takes the parser's nodes out, which answers the critique's hole on the parser's node; deciders 2 and 3 kept those nodes inside the code, which holds only until that work lands. The measure finds node in many senses today, Node, the parser's tree and the tree of names, and member as a rival of node in the files naming `manifest`, so the one sense follows from the two removals.


**Cost.** No case, refusal or word of the surface. [QSSLF#axis], #near and #page at the move; the pages of `binding`, `source`, `doc`, `runLaws`, `laws`, `tutorial` and `explain` in `core/lib/qlang/any.qlang` with their laws (`:readsAMemberByAnAnchor`, `:UnknownNameError/refusesAnAnchorNoMemberAnswers`, `:UnknownNameError/namesTheMembersNear` and their kin, renamed by their rule), and “membership” in `set.qlang` and `vec.qlang`; `memberRecordOf` of `core/src/runtime/axis.mjs` and `nearestMembers` of `core/src/nearest-names.mjs` named for the node, `isMemberValue` of `core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs` and `core/src/runtime/verb.mjs` named `isVerbValue` and `callMemberOn` of `core/src/eval.mjs` and `core/src/runtime/print.mjs` named `callVerbOn`; the parser's node with [D339] part 1; [D294]'s text says the anchor of a node. member enters the lexicon [D350] once no text that states what holds writes it.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «и там где-то в доках было про что стоит подальше держаться от ООП и т.п. объектовых абстракций..» (maintainer, 2026-10-08 21:27, session 22b934f8); «а то что у нас в проекте не было и даже порицилось использование всякой классической объектно-ориентированной замусоренной терминологии в виде класса и объекта и т.п..» (2026-09-23 08:28, 86982eb5).

## 5. Chain, signature and commands

Agreement split, size M.

**Road.** Chain is the one word for the commands that build a function or a kind, and signature leaves; the refusal is named for what it refuses, a command its noun does not take as written, and a payload that is no pair of a chain and a recipe goes to the refusal of the pair.


**Decision.** A function and a kind are built from their noun by a chain, the commands written on it, each a verb whose subject is the function or the kind so far, `::fn | takes … | impl …`; the literal folds the chain into a quote beside the recipe, `::fn[~(takes :by |~~ How much. ~~| /0) ~(add by)]`, a kind's beside its check or null, and `manifest` answers the roles of the chain. A payload that is no pair of the quote of a chain and a recipe or a check is refused as `…/PayloadNotPairError`, `::kind[[] null] !| type` answering `::kind/PayloadNotPairError`. A step of the chain its noun does not take as written, a call of no command of the noun or a command written with args its verb does not take, `returns #[]` or `takes :true`, is refused as `::fn/CommandNotTakenError` and `::kind/CommandNotTakenError`, `::fn | returns |~~ A. ~~| #[] !| type` answering `::fn/CommandNotTakenError`, the page of each command telling the args it takes; where those args are spelled once is [D272]'s. Signature names nothing of the language; the language server's signature help, a request of its protocol, keeps its name.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: the first page, the head of [QSBLD] and its section `## chain` say chain, the page of `::fn` and the code signature, and [QSBLD#fn] already writes “the chain folded into a quote”, so the two words name one thing in its two forms and a reader who wrote a chain meets the chain in its refusal. Words carry paradigms: signature wakes the types of a function's parameters, which a quote of pages, laws, invariants and facts is more than. [QSERR#site]: a last name says what was refused, and the critique's counterexample, `returns #[]` and `takes :true` refused as no commands, is cured by naming the command not taken, which stays exact whether [D272] reads the args of each command from its verb or from one table; the pages of `PayloadNotPairError` already define the pair as the commands and the recipe.


**Rationale.** Deciders 1 and 2 chose chain; decider 3 kept chain and signature for two things, the commands written and the quote held, which the texts already call one, “the chain folded into a quote” ([QSBLD#fn]), so chain takes both forms (2/3). On the refusal all three parted: `ChainNotCommandsError` (decider 1) still says “not commands” of commands written right; keeping `SignatureNotCommandsError` (decider 3) keeps the word that leaves; `CommandNotTakenError` (decider 2) is exact for every step it refuses, `::fn[~(add 1) ~(add 1)]`, `::fn[~(returns) ~(1)]` and the laws of `fn.qlang`, and a first that is no quote, `::fn[[] ~(1)]`, is what the page of `PayloadNotPairError` already describes, “The literal of a function is a pair, the commands of its signature and its recipe”. Decided by [QSERR#site] and by no turn back: the name holds whichever road [D272] takes. The measure gives no edge between the two words, each beside `impl` in most of its matches.


**Cost.** `::fn/SignatureNotCommandsError` and `::kind/SignatureNotCommandsError` renamed `…/CommandNotTakenError` with their pages and laws (`core/lib/qlang/fn.qlang`, `core/lib/qlang/kind.qlang`) and their cases (QSBLD/fn, QSBLD/kind, QSBLD/answer), the law `:SignatureNotCommandsError/refusesASignatureThatIsNoQuote` moving under `PayloadNotPairError` with the probe of this record; [QSBLD#fn], #answer (“`::fn/SignatureNotCommandsError` an empty set”) and #kind at the move; the first page; `readSignature`, `signatureOf`, `EMPTY_SIGNATURE`, `readKindSignature` and the module `core/src/runtime/signature.mjs` named for the chain, with their importers (`core/src/eval.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/fn.mjs`, `nouns.mjs`, `verb.mjs`, `kind.mjs`, `lsp/src/features.mjs`) and the text of [D340] part 1 that names the module; [D272]'s text says the chain and the args each of its commands takes. signature and `SignatureNotCommandsError` enter the lexicon [D350], the protocol's signature help aside. A host that catches the refusal renames it in one release.


**Removes.** Flagged: the last name `SignatureNotCommandsError` of `::fn` and `::kind`, renamed `CommandNotTakenError`, and a first that is no quote refused as `PayloadNotPairError`; no capability.


**The maintainer's words.** None chooses chain or signature for the commands of a function: «и может надо сигнатуру (контракт) описывать отдельно, а содержимое реализации отдельно, если там реально разые реализации ещё ..» (maintainer, 2026-09-25 17:32, session 86982eb5) is an idea of several implementations under one contract, before the chain; «и что все это сплошная цепочка команд» (2026-10-11 23:02, 22b934f8) speaks of the catalog as one chain of commands.

But the clause «a command written with args its verb does not take, `returns #[]` or `takes :true`» is false. `::fn/returns` declares `:kinds #[::set ::tag]` and `::fn/takes` declares `:name ::keyword`, so the verbs take `#[]` and `:true`. What refuses them is the chain's own table (core/src/runtime/signature.mjs:27-39): `isKinds` asks for a set of one tag at least, and `isInputName` refuses a word of a literal. **Repair.** Say «a command written with args the rules of its chain do not take, `returns #[]` or `takes :true`», with no claim that the verb refuses them. Where those rules are spelled stays D272's question.


## 6. A host's implementation and the host

Agreement 2/3, size M.

**Road.** Host for every tool that hands the language its sources, the core first; primitive for the JavaScript function a host hands under an address whose recipe is `::builtin{}`; register for writing the core's primitives into its table; the runtime for the evaluator with that table; `impl`, `::builtin{}` and the recipe kept; implementation, kernel and the host's bind leave; the engine and the terminal named so.


**Decision.** A host is a tool that hands the language its sources, each `{ uri, source, primitives }`, and receives its answers: the core is the host of the language's own sources, and the command line, the language server, the site and the sister project are hosts, so a verb of the core and a verb of the command line answer alike, each `::builtin{}` [QSNAM#owner]. `impl` gives a function its recipe, code or `::builtin{}`, the descriptor of a recipe a host answers by a primitive, the JavaScript function it hands under the verb's address beside its source; the runtime, the evaluator with its table of primitives and the values it reads, `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`, runs it, and a failure inside a primitive is `::call/HostFailureError`. The modules of the runtime register the core's primitives in that table, two under one address refused as `::builtin/AddressRegisteredTwiceError`; the root refuses a `::builtin{}` its host handed no primitive for as `::builtin/PrimitiveNotHandedError` and a primitive handed for a name declared otherwise as `::builtin/PrimitiveNamesNoBuiltinError`; a loader's refusal names the platform it ran on, `:platform` of `::builtin/SourceLoadError`, `:node` or `:web`. The engine a host runs on bounds the length of a vector and runs a regex alike on every platform, the terminal hands the REPL a paste in chunks, and the painters read the verbs of the root, `verbNames`.


**Why.** One model: the core is a host of its own sources [QSHST#source] and a word of the host answers as any [QSNAM#owner], so its verbs take no second name, the critique's conflict with the road that named them the runtime's. One fact, one spelling and [D349]: in the files naming `::builtin` the runtime stands in most matches and kernel in a few, implementation duplicates primitive and the command `impl`, and the host's field `impls` meets the command `impl` in every search; bind is the language's word for a declaration, `::bind` and `::binding`, while the table the core's modules write into is a registry, `PRIMITIVE_REGISTRY` and `createPrimitiveRegistry`, whose own verb is register [D351]; host keeps the embedding tool, the platform and the engine taking their own words. `::builtin` stays, the maintainer's example of a word that strikes its domain, and `builtinNames` leaves it for the verbs of the root (part 3).


**Rationale.** All three keep `::builtin`, `impl` and the recipe, name the JavaScript function a primitive and keep the core a host of its own sources. Deciders 1 and 2 rename `impls` and let kernel go, decider 3 keeps both for stability; the 2/3 road stands by [D349] and by the measure of the files naming `::builtin`, where kernel is a rival of the runtime. On the refusal of two primitives under one address the three parted (`PrimitiveHandedTwiceError`, `AddressHandedTwiceError`, `AddressRegisteredTwiceError`): the defect is raised by the table the core's modules write into, `createPrimitiveRegistry` in `core/src/primitives.mjs`, no host's `primitives` holding one address twice, so register, the registry's own verb, names it (decider 3, by [D351]'s consistency), and hand stays the act of a host at the boundary. The fact `:host` (decider 1) holds `:node` or `:web`, a platform as its page says; deciders 2 and 3 named its value without choosing the fact's name, and [D349] leaves host one sense.


**Cost.** The host's field `impls` renamed `primitives` in `core/src/runtime/root.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/index.mjs`, `core/src/session.mjs`, `cli/src/cli-sources.mjs`, `core/README.md`, [QSHST#source] and the text of [D293] and [D340]; the refusals `::builtin/ImplementationNotHandedError`, `::builtin/ImplementationNamesNoBuiltinError` and `::builtin/AddressBoundTwiceError` renamed `::builtin/PrimitiveNotHandedError`, `::builtin/PrimitiveNamesNoBuiltinError` and `::builtin/AddressRegisteredTwiceError` with their pages (`core/lib/qlang/builtin.qlang`), their messages (“bind:” in `core/src/primitives.mjs`) and the unit tests that read them (`core/test/unit/primitives.test.mjs`, `session.test.mjs`); the fact `:host` of `::builtin/SourceLoadError` renamed `:platform` in `core/src/source-load-error.mjs` and its page, which already says “the platform”; the head of `core/lib/qlang/builtin.qlang` (“kernel”) and [QSHST#source] (the kernel, implementation, binds, a plain function), [QSHST#repl] (“A paste a host hands over” said of the terminal), [QSERR#site] (“an invariant of the implementation”, “a host's implementation”), the pages of `::number/range` (“a vector of the host”) and `::regex` (“every host reads alike”) said of the engine, `::call/HostFailureError` (a host's primitive), `cli/src/line-editor.mjs` and the head of `core/src/runtime/root.mjs` (“reaches an implementation”); the case `an-invariant-of-the-kernel-sits-under-builtin` of QSERR/site renamed by its rule; `bindPrim` and `bindStateReader` named for registering across `core/src/runtime/`, and `HOST_IMPLS`, `hostImplOf` and `declareHostValue` of `core/src/types.mjs` for the primitive; the aim's “A primitive enters”, which means a verb, says verb. kernel, `impls` and the retired last names enter the lexicon [D350]. The sister project's sources hand `impls` and rename it in one release.


**Removes.** Flagged: the host's field `impls`, renamed `primitives`; the last names `ImplementationNotHandedError`, `ImplementationNamesNoBuiltinError` and `AddressBoundTwiceError`, renamed; the fact `:host` of a loader's refusal, renamed `:platform`. No capability: `::builtin`, `impl` and the recipe stay.

**Checker: refuted.** Several things stand: primitive for the JavaScript function, register for the table, the core as a host of its own sources, `::builtin` and `impl` kept, and `:platform` for `:node` or `:web`. ## 7. A name of the package that says what its value no longer is

Agreement 3/3, size S.

**Road.** `typeKeyword` renamed for what it answers, `typeOf`, after the verb `type`.


**Decision.** `typeOf(value)`, exported by the core, answers the tag of a value's kind, what `type` answers: its outermost tag, or the kind of the core its literal implies, `[1 2] | type | type` answering `::tag`.


**Why.** [QSWRK#code]: the code names each thing as the language names it, and the verb is `type`; the name kept the layer where a kind was a keyword (`git show e5ba5dbb:core/src/types.mjs`), which the maintainer read as a leak.


**Rationale.** All three rename it `typeOf`; decider 3 found the maintainer's word on the leak, which the decision quotes.

A host that imports it renames the import in one release. No case.

## 8. Shape, and outline

Agreement 2/3, size M.

**Road.** Shape names one thing, the structure of a value, which `shape` answers under `::shape` and a template writes; outline and schema leave the shape, schema keeping the fields of a record of a step; every other sense of shape takes its own word.


**Decision.** The shape of a value is its structure beneath its tags, the kind at each of its parts, the keys of its maps and the shape of its elements: `shape` answers it as a value of `::shape`, `[1] | shape` answering `::shape[::number]`; a template of `get` writes the shape of an answer, a path or a quote at each place, `get {:who /first}`, and a task that asks an answer of a shape asks the same thing. `paths` answers the leaves of a value, each the pair of the path that reads it and its kind. Outline names nothing of the language, and schema only the fields a record of a step holds [QSQUO#step]. Every other sense takes its word: the walk of a subject's tags ends at the kind of the core beneath them, the keys of a map travel with its values, the runtime reads the values `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`, a value comes apart into its atoms and the template that builds it back [QSLIT#atoms], and the grammar writes a location in the form of `location()`.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling and Words carry paradigms: the shape `shape` answers, the shape a template writes and the shape a task of the benchmark asks, “Expected shape: a JSON array of strings”, are one structure, read and written, which answers the maintainer's question whether the verb's shape and a literal's are two things; he named the answer `::shape`, and his outline of an arbitrary value was what `shape` became. Outline named the answer of `paths` in the catalog and that of `shape` in the aim, two things under one word, and schema a third word beside them; borrow concepts, not formats, keeps JSON Schema out of the word.


**Rationale.** Deciders 2 and 3 let outline and schema go, `paths` answering the leaves of a value; decider 1 kept outline for `paths`. The maintainer's own words give outline to what `shape` answers and call the answer of `paths` a schema, so outline kept for `paths` would stand against his sense of it, and the measure puts outline and schema as rivals of shape in the files naming `::shape`, shape holding nine in ten of their matches. The 2/3 road stands; schema keeps the one sense `PayloadNotSchemaError` gives it (part 13).

**Road.** The maintainer's direction: frame names the frame of a trace alone; the count of nesting the evaluator keeps is the evaluation depth, the name its refusal carries; depth alone the nesting of data, budget the budget of a print.


**Decision.** A frame is one frame of a trace, a value of `::frame`: the subject a step met, the steps of its frame from the one that broke, and the place of the part its code broke on, `::frame[subject steps place]`, the trace holding one for each step a failure left. The count of nesting the evaluator keeps is the evaluation depth: a step, a call, a modifier its call computes, a literal, a group and each run of the code a verb runs evaluate one deeper than the step that called them, and a call past the limit is refused as `::call/EvaluationDepthExceededError`, its `:depth` the evaluation depth refused and its `:limit` the deepest one evaluation goes, `:number/inf ~(add 1 | inf) | 0 | inf !| get [~(type) /limit]` answering `[::call/EvaluationDepthExceededError 1000]`. Depth alone names the nesting of data or of an address, and budget the budget of a print [QSPRN#elide]; frame names no level of the evaluation, and level no frame of a trace.

One fact, one spelling.


**Rationale.** The three deciders kept the text 3/3, one paradigm of a stack of calls counting the depth and the trace in one kind of level, the critique's road; none read the maintainer's answers of 12:56 and 13:01, which weighed that very reading and chose otherwise: `::frame` keeps one thing and the plain frame of the evaluation takes an anchor term of its own. Those answers bind, and the evaluation depth is the term he accepted at 13:10 after the session measured depth, budget, level and stack as heaps of senses; the deciders' argument, one paradigm, is the one the measure of D351 shows split into three rivals, so it does not outweigh his choice.

## 11. Source, uri and origin

**Road.** Source for code as written; origin, a keyword, for where a text came from on every value that names it; `:uri` leaves the facts, the host's field `uri` stays.


**Decision.** A source is code as written: the text a host hands as `{ uri, source, primitives }`, the step that declared a binding, its `:source`, every declaration under an address, which the axis `source` reads, and the line where a parse stopped, the `:source` of `::quote/ParseError` while [D339] keeps it. ## 12. Parse, both ways

Agreement 2/3, size M.

**Road.** `parse` reads text alone; `toString` writes the text of a quote's code as it writes an atom's text; `::quote/parse` leaves.


**Decision.** `parse` reads text alone, into the quote it writes, `"add 1" | parse` answering `~(add 1)`, a blank source the empty quote and a source that reads as no pipeline refused as `::quote/ParseError`. `toString` writes the text a value stands for: a number as JSON writes it, a keyword and a tag name by their names, a boolean and null by their literals, and a quote by its code, its steps in the one spelling the printer writes and without its brackets, `~(add 1 | mul 2) | toString` answering `"add 1 | mul 2"` and `"5 |  mul   2" | parse | toString` answering `"5 | mul 2"`, which `parse` reads back as the quote, so a quote turned into text and back is itself, as a number, a keyword, a string and a tag are. `print` writes the literal, `~(add 1) | print` answering `"~(add 1)"`, `json` the JSON of that literal, and `parseJson` reads JSON; the texts of a vector, a set and a map stay `print` and `json`, and the text of a doc its `content`.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms: parse wakes reading text into structure, and one name that reads text on a string and writes it on a quote turns the habit around; the text of code is the habit of `toString`, the source a function's `toString` gives in the language most readers know, and the conversions of the language are words named `to` and the kind they answer, which the maintainer named among them. The aim's one measure, the first attempt, is served where the habit lands, and the round trip of a conversion then holds of a quote by `toString` and `parse`.


**Rationale.** Deciders 2 and 3 give a quote's text to `toString`, decider 1 to `content`, the word that answers a doc's text, `toString` refusing a quote as a container. The 2/3 road stands: the first attempt the aim measures lands on `toString`, and the round trip of [QSCND#convert] extends to a quote. Decider 1's road, one word for the text inside a doc's and a quote's brackets, is set aside: it holds [QSCND#convert]'s rule that a container's texts are `print` and `json` (a quote is a vector of steps, [QSQUO#step]) at the cost of a round trip to a refusal for every reader who reaches for `toString`, and a doc's prose and a quote's code are two texts a reader asks for apart.


**Cost.** `::quote/parse` leaves, `::quote/toString` taking its page and its laws (`core/lib/qlang/quote.qlang`, the law of `parse | parse` written as `toString | parse`) and its primitive (`core/src/runtime/code.mjs`); every text that writes a quote's text by `parse`: the first page's law `:errors/holdsTheSkippedSteps`, `source | parse` in `core/lib/qlang/any.qlang`, the pages of the trace and the frame, the cases of [QSQUO#parse] and those that read a source's text (QSERR/site, QSNAM/address, QSSLF/axis, QSDSP/refusal), the probe of [QSHST#source] (`::vec/count | source | toString | contains "::builtin{}"`); [QSQUO#parse] and [QSCND#convert] (“a quote by its code” and its round trip) at the move. The `:nearest` of `toString` on a vector, a set and a map stays `[:json :print]`. The sister project's queries that call `parse` on a quote write `toString`.


**Removes.** Flagged: the verb `::quote/parse`, its act kept by `::quote/toString`; reading text by `parse` stays.


**The maintainer's words.** «или конвенции, а ля toString toMap toVec toNumber toTag..» (maintainer, 2026-10-12 18:47, session 014e5a04); «для меня это просто забавный эффект и не более...» (18:36), of an involution of the language held for its own sake; «модели тоже страдают от того что язык не предлагает им прямого какого-то пути -  выучил в одно месте - пользуешься по аналогии во всех других» (18:36).

## 13. The refusal of what a holder takes is named two ways

Agreement 3/3, size S.

**Road.** The record's road for the records of the steps; the two refusals of a subject and of a holder kept as the two rules of [QSERR#site].


**Decision.** A value of none of the kinds a holder takes is refused by the holder's role, its kinds the fact `:expected`: an input, an answer, a payload, an element, the subject of a function `apply` runs, `…/SubjectKindNotTakenError`, and the payload of a record of a step, `::call`, `::bind`, `::construct`, `::fail` and `::group` alike, `…/PayloadKindNotTakenError`, `get [~(::call[1] !| get [~(type) /expected]) ~(::fail[1] !| get [~(type) /expected])]` answering `[[::call/PayloadKindNotTakenError #[::map]] [::fail/PayloadKindNotTakenError #[::quote]]]`. A map whose fields break the schema of its record is refused as `…/PayloadNotSchemaError`, its `:field` the field, `::call{:name 1} !| get /field` answering `:name`. The subject of a verb is refused by the label it resides on, `…/SubjectNot<Label>Error`, with no `:expected`, `"x" | add 1 !| type` answering `::number/add/SubjectNotNumberError`.


**Why.** [QSERR#site]: residence and the kinds of a holder are two rules with facts of their own, the critique's counterexample, so two names; One fact, one spelling: a payload of another kind is one refusal on every record of a step, where `:field` null named nothing and the page spoke of “no Map”, a word of the JavaScript.


**Rationale.** All three keep the two rules of [QSERR#site] apart and refuse a payload of another kind by the role of the holder with `:expected`; the probes on the tree answer `::call/PayloadNotSchemaError` with `:field` null for `::call[1]`, `::bind[1]` and `::construct[1]`, and `:field :name` for `::call{:name 1}`, which keeps its refusal.


**Cost.** The refusal of a payload that is no map under `::call`, `::bind` and `::construct`, `recordConstructor` in `core/src/runtime/quote-steps.mjs`; the pages of `call.qlang`, `bind.qlang` and `construct.qlang` each gaining the refusal; [QSQUO#step] at the move and a case under it in QSQUO/step. With [D339] part 1 the schema of the steps is what makes data a quote.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «мне главное имя уникальное для сигнала иметь» (maintainer, 2026-10-09 17:17, session 22b934f8).

**Checker: stands.** The two rules of [QSERR#site] stay apart. Refusing the payload of every record of a step by its role, with `:expected`, makes one refusal of what `::fail`, `::group` and `::error` already answer, and a map's field keeps `PayloadNotSchemaError`. It lands on the pages and in the [QSQUO#step] it names.

**Probe.** qlang '::call[1] !| get [~(type) /field]' → [::call/PayloadNotSchemaError null]; qlang '::bind[1] !| get [~(type) /field]' → [::bind/PayloadNotSchemaError null]; qlang '::group[1] !| get [~(type) /expected]' → [::group/PayloadKindNotTakenError #[::quote]]; qlang '::error[1] !| get [~(type) /expected]' → [::error/PayloadKindNotTakenError #[::map]]; qlang '::call{:name 1} !| get [~(type) /field]' → [::call/PayloadNotSchemaError :name]

## 14. Place, index and key

Agreement split, size M.

**Road.** Index for the number of an element's position, key for the entry of a map, segment for what a path reads where a refusal names neither, place for a path that locates a part.


**Decision.** An index is the number of an element's position in a vector, or in a set in its one order, from zero, a negative index counted from the end, as `:index` and `::path/IndexOutOfBoundsError` hold; a key names an entry of a map, `::path/KeyNotInMapError` with `:key` and the `:key` of a refusal at an entry; a segment is a key or an index a path reads, which `segments` answers. A refusal of a path at a segment that reads neither an index of its vector nor a key of a map names the segment, `::path/IndexNotIntegerError` and `::path/SubjectHasNoPartsError` with `:segment`, `[10 20] | get /name !| get [~(type) /segment]` answering `[::path/IndexNotIntegerError :name]`. A place is a path that locates a part: the place of the args an input reads, `/0` or `~(query /0 1)`, the place a frame names of the part a verb's code broke on, `trace | last | place` answering `/1`, and the place of the payload a field reads. Vectors of one length whose elements part index by index are tuples (part 30).


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: the facts say index and key in their one senses, the position of an element and the entry of a map, `::map/filter/ConditionNotBooleanError` naming by `:key` the entry it refused, while the two refusals of a path held as `:key` the segment they read, `:name` read from a vector or any segment read from an atom; segment is the word of [QSPTH] and of `segments` for what a path reads, and place keeps the one sense the verbs `place` and `takes` give it.


**Rationale.** Decider 1 named the fact `:segment`, decider 2 `:index`, decider 3 kept `:key`. `:index` holding `:name`, a keyword, would put the word of a number over a name; `:key` keeps a second sense beside the key of a map that the refusals at an entry hold (`{:a 1} | filter ~(add 1) !| keys` holds `:key`); `:segment` says what was read, the term `segments` answers. Decided by One fact, one spelling. The doc's `segments`, the word's other sense, is [D339] part 7's to take or keep; with it the word has one sense.


**Cost.** The facts `:key` of `::path/IndexNotIntegerError` and `::path/SubjectHasNoPartsError` renamed `:segment` with their pages, their laws and the cases that read one (QSPTH); `core/src/path-read.mjs`; the prose that says place for the position of an element: [QSPTH#value], #get and #words, [QSSLF#axis] (“the place in the set”) and [QSLIT#atoms] at the move, the pages of `::path` (“A vector is read by a place”), `::error/index`, `segments`, `set.qlang` (“naming its place in the one order”), `desc.qlang` and `vec.qlang`, the law `:readsTuplesPlaceByPlace`, and `makePath` of `core/src/types.mjs` (“a place of a vector”). The words are those [D344] part 2 weighs for where a failure broke.


**Removes.** Flagged: the fact `:key` of two refusals of a path, renamed `:segment`.


**The maintainer's words.** None on this axis.

**Checker: refuted.** `:segment` for the two refusals of a path stands. Two defects remain. (1) The definition «A place is a path that locates a part» is contradicted by its own example `~(query /0 1)`, which is code ([QSBLD#input]: «or code over the args»). It is also contradicted by [QSPRN#span] «The place a reading of text stopped is a span» and by [QSERR#site] «Every place that refuses has a tag of its own». (2) The position sense of place stays in sections and pages its list leaves out: [QSORD#sort] «a vector key ranking place by place» (:76-77), [QSORD#desc] «one place of a vector key» (:106), [QSPTH#key] «a vector as a place» (:105), [QSPTH#write] «a place a vector holds» (:274, :278), the page of `capture` «`match` answers the groups by their places» (string.qlang:1252), and the page of `cond` «place in the args, from zero» (any.qlang:684). It also stays in case names such as `desc-turns-one-place-of-a-vector-key-to-descend`, `a-vector-key-ranks-by-its-next-place-among-ties`, `get-refuses-a-place-past-the-end` and `a-path-reads-a-key-then-a-place-then-a-key`. Place stays out of the lexicon, so nothing catches these.

## 15. Marker, mark, absent, each and address

Agreement 2/3, size M.

**Road.** Elision for a value of `::elision`, caret for the line drawn under a failure to parse, mark for the tags of a shape, absent for `::absent` alone, `::each` kept and its section of [QSCOL] renamed image, email for the key a shape folds.


**Decision.** A part a view left out gives way to an elision, a value of `::elision`, whose `:size` counts what it stands for and whose `:read` continues the query to it. A failure to parse draws a caret under its place as `:caret`, beside its `:source`, while [D339] keeps both, `"[1 2" | parse !| get [/source /location ~(get /caret | trim)]` answering `["[1 2" ::span[4 1 5 4 1 5] "^"]`. The tags of a shape over a part are its marks, `::absent` over a key some records lack and `::nullable` over a part null in some of the data, and a map whose keys are data folds into `::each` over its first key, a key that reads as an id, a date, a time, a uuid or an email among them; `::null` is the kind of null, the value that stands for none, and absent names the key a record lacks alone. The section of [QSCOL] that tells the code `map` runs on each element and the images it answers is [QSCOL#image].


**Why.** One fact, one spelling and [D351]: a value of a kind takes the kind's word, as a frame is a value of `::frame`, and in the files naming `::elision` marker and elision stood as rivals for one thing, which elision, the kind's own word and the maintainer's элизия, takes; marker then names nothing of the language, and the caret takes its exact word, the markers of a bracketed paste being the terminal's. Absent and address each had a second sense, null and an email; `::absent` and `::each` are the maintainer's words, so the section moves to the word of what `map` answers, the images, which the chapter already writes in one sense.


**Rationale.** On the value: deciders 2 and 3 call it an elision (decider 2 adding marker as a gloss), decider 1 a marker; the measure shows the two as rivals in the files naming `::elision`, and [D351] keeps the kind's word. On the fact: deciders 1 and 2 rename `:marker` to `:caret`, decider 3 keeps it; with marker leaving the elision, the caret is the exact word. Mark stays for the tags of a shape (deciders 1 and 2). The section `## each` moves (deciders 1 and 2), to image (decider 2), the chapter's word of one sense for what `map` answers, where mapping, decider 1's, would wake a map, the kind `## map` of the same chapter tells.


**Cost.** The fact `:marker` of `::quote/ParseError` renamed `:caret` with its page, [QSSYN#refusal], [QSQUO#parse], [QSHST#answer] and [QSERR#print] at the move, the cases of QSLIT/number and QSSYN that read it and `core/src/error-convert.mjs`, unless [D339] part 6 takes the fact out; the texts that call an elision a marker, the pages of `::elision` and `elide` (`core/lib/qlang/elision.qlang`, `any.qlang`), the first page, `cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang`, [QSPRN#print], [QSPRN#elide], [QSHST#answer], [QSPTH#words] and [QSSLF#page]; the page of `::null` (“the kind of the absent value”); `core/lib/qlang/each.qlang` and `core/src/runtime/path.mjs` (an address read as an email); the section `## each` of [QSCOL] renamed `## image`, its file `core/test/conformance/QSCOL/each.jsonl` renamed `image.jsonl` and the citation of `core/src/runtime/vec.mjs`; [D327]'s text says elision.


**Removes.** Flagged: the fact `:marker` of a failure to parse, renamed `:caret`; the section anchor [QSCOL#each], renamed [QSCOL#image].

## 16. Anchor and name, right and left

Agreement 2/3, size S.

**Road.** `:anchor` for the name of a node every axis takes; `:condition` for the boolean `and` and `or` take, as `if` takes one; right and left leave; the comparisons' `:value` and the algebra's `:other` stay.


**Decision.** An axis that takes the name of a node under its subject takes it as `:anchor`, a keyword, the last name of that node, `binding`, `doc`, `runLaws` and `source` alike, `::vec/take | get [~(source 1 !| type) ~(binding 1 !| type)]` answering `[::any/source/anchor/KindNotTakenError ::any/binding/anchor/KindNotTakenError]`; which axes take one is [D294]'s. `and` and `or` join the subject and the condition they take, `true | and (5 | gt 3)`, as `if` takes its `:condition`, a value of another kind refused as `::boolean/and/condition/KindNotTakenError`, `true | and 1 !| type` answering it; their pages name the subject and the condition, no left and no right. A comparison takes the `:value` it compares with and the algebra the `:other` set or map.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: one input, one name across the axes, the first page's anchor, the word of hypertext, and one name for the boolean a verb reads, which `if` already calls its `:condition`. Words carry paradigms: right and left are the words of an infix operator, where a step has a subject and the args of its verb; [D349]: other is the most generic of the three words in our texts.


**Rationale.** All three take `:anchor` for every axis that takes a name; deciders 1 and 3 give `and` and `or` a `:condition`, decider 2 an `:other`. `if` already takes a `:condition`, `::any/if | binding | get /value | params | map ~(get /name)` answering `[:condition :then :else]`, so the boolean a verb reads keeps one name, and other stays the algebra's set or map.


**Cost.** `::any/source/name/KindNotTakenError` renamed `::any/source/anchor/KindNotTakenError` with the input, its page and its law (`core/lib/qlang/any.qlang`, `core/src/runtime/axis.mjs`); the inputs `:right` of `::boolean/and` and `::boolean/or` renamed `:condition` with their refusals, pages and laws (`core/lib/qlang/boolean.qlang`, “the left”, “the right”); [QSSLF#axis] and [QSCND#boolean] at the move with the cases that name the refusals (QSSLF/axis, QSCND/boolean); this record's probe.


**Removes.** Flagged: the inputs `:name` of `source` and `:right` of `and` and `or`, renamed with their refusals; a query writes its modifiers by position, so none breaks, while a test that catches the old address does.


**The maintainer's words.** None on this axis.


**Files.** In D:/git/qlang: core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, boolean.qlang, core/src/runtime/axis.mjs, docs/spec/QSSLF-the-language-describes-itself.md, QSCND-conditions-and-conversions.md, core/test/conformance/QSSLF/, QSCND/


**Checker: refuted.** `:anchor` for the name every axis takes, and `:condition` for `and` and `or`, stand; `if` already takes `[:condition :then :else]`. But the road «right and left leave», on its own reasoning that these are the words of an infix operator, is contradicted by two facts the record keeps without a word: `:leftType` and `:rightType` of `KindsMismatchedError`. They are declared under `::error` as «The kind of the value on the left» and «on the right», and [QSCOL#algebra] states them.

**Probe.** qlang '[#[1] {:a 1}] | union !| get [~(type) /leftType /rightType]' → [::vec/union/KindsMismatchedError ::set ::map]; qlang '[::error/leftType ::error/rightType] | map ~doc' → the pages «The kind of the value on the left.» and «The kind of the value on the right.»; qlang '::any/if | binding | get /value | params | map ~(get /name)' → [:condition :then :else]; qlang 'true | and 1 !| type' → ::boolean/and/right/KindNotTakenError; qlang '::vec/take | get [~(source 1 !| type) ~(binding 1 !| type)]' → [::any/source/name/KindNotTakenError ::any/binding/anchor/KindNotTakenError]

**Repair.** Scope the road to the inputs and pages of `and` and `or`. Or carry it to the two facts and to [QSCOL#algebra], which changes facts and is flagged as a removal.


## 17. The kinds of the steps

Agreement 2/3, size S.

**Road.** `::invariant` the kind of an invariant, as `::law` is the kind of a law, the command `holds` kept for the act; `::bind`, `::construct`, its `:payload`, `construct/construct`, `::fail` and `::group` kept, each with the sentence that defines it.


**Decision.** An invariant is a query the values of a kind or of an input keep, a quote under `::invariant`, which the command `holds` writes in a chain, as a law is a quote under `::law`, which `proves` writes: `::vec/take/count/NotIntegerError | binding | get /value | type` answers `::invariant`. A step is stored as the record of its form [QSQUO#step]: a call as `::call`, a declaration as `::bind`, which writes a `::binding` into its scope, a tagged literal as `::construct`, whose `:payload` is the payload the literal writes and whose constructor is `::construct/construct` as every label's is `construct`, a step of the fail track, which catches a failure, as `::fail`, and a group as `::group`, `~(:x 1 | ::Box[1] !| 0) | map ~type` answering `[::bind ::construct ::fail]`.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms: a tag names a kind, a thing, and an invariant is a thing, the word of the prose, of `INVARIANT_TAG` and of the maintainer's ask, while holds, the act of its command, is the commonest verb of the texts [D349]; the command keeps the act, as `proves` writes a `::law`. The other records each have a sentence that defines them: `::fail` names the step of the fail track that `!|` opens, which [QSSYN] and [QSERR] call the fail track, and `::bind` the maintainer's binding steps.


**Rationale.** Deciders 1 and 2 rename `::holds` to `::invariant`, decider 3 keeps it for stability; the maintainer asked for `::invariant` and left the choice to the model, and the measure decides it: holds stands in a thousand matches of the texts as a plain verb, invariant in the files naming `holds` almost wholly. Decider 2 alone renamed `::fail` to `::catch`; deciders 1 and 3 keep it, the tag naming the track the step stands on and catch staying the act of `!|` [QSERR#catch].


**Cost.** The tag `::holds` renamed `::invariant`: its source `core/lib/qlang/holds.qlang` renamed `invariant.qlang`, the first page's chapter of verbs (`#[::any ::fn ::holds ::kind]`), [QSBLD#label] and #chain (`:Positive ::holds~(gt 0)`) and [QSQUO#step] at the move, `INVARIANT_TAG` of `core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs` and `core/src/eval.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/bootstrap.mjs`, `cli/test/error-tag-catalog-drift.test.mjs` (`isLaw`), the cases that write `::holds` and the probes of [D339] parts 9 and 10; data a reader wrote under `::holds` reads anew. One sentence each on the pages of `::bind`, `::construct`, `::fail` and `::law`.


**Removes.** Flagged: the tag `::holds`, renamed `::invariant`; the command `holds` stays.


**The maintainer's words.** «слушай, может все таки сделать ::snippet и ::invariant ?   ... или почему мы отказываемся от них?..» (maintainer, 2026-09-28 14:06, session d427ce7e); «а ок, выберий какой-то достойный вариант и делай» (2026-10-09 19:30, 22b934f8), on the model's card that kept `::holds` and `::law`; «у нас c обычного двоеточия будут начинаться фактически все биндинг шаги» (2026-10-08 00:23, 22b934f8).

## 18. A refusal named for a rule that left

Agreement 3/3, size S.

**Road.** The key refused by the role of the holder with the kinds a key files under as `:expected`, the composite names of `minus` and `inter` joining it.


**Decision.** `groupBy`, `indexBy` and `toMap` file an element under the keyword of its key, a keyword as it is and a string, a number, a boolean, null or a tag name under the keyword `toKeyword` answers for it [QSCND#convert]; a key of another kind is refused at its element as `…/KeyKindNotTakenError`, with its `:index` and, as `:expected`, the kinds a key files under, spelled once beside the rule that files them, `[{:k [1]}] | indexBy /k !| get [~(type) /expected]` answering `[::vec/indexBy/KeyKindNotTakenError #[::boolean ::keyword ::null ::number ::string ::tag]]`. The keys `minus` and `inter` take off a map are keywords or strings, another refused as `…/KeyKindNotTakenError` with `:expected #[::keyword ::string]`, `{:a 1} | minus [[1]] !| type` answering `::map/minus/KeyKindNotTakenError`.


**Why.** [QSERR#site]: a value of none of the kinds a role takes is refused by the role with its kinds; One fact, one spelling: the kinds a key files under are spelled once; the old name kept the rule of a key that was a keyword (e48e2a6c), and `KeyNotKeywordOrStringError` is the composite name the maintainer called brittle.


**Rationale.** All three name the refusal by the role with the kinds a key files under as `:expected`; the critique's road, refusing as `toKeyword` refuses, is set aside by all, since it answers `::call/KindAnswersNoVerbError` for a verb the reader never called. Decider 2 alone carried the rule to `minus` and `inter`, whose composite last name is the kind the maintainer called brittle; it is taken so one rule has one name, and their facts already hold `:index`.

## 21. How the language is introduced

Agreement 2/3, size S.

## 22. The path of a failure

Agreement 3/3, size L.

**Road.** The maintainer's direction: the path of a failure is a trace of frames, the fact `:trace`, the verb `trace` and the kind `::trace`; path names a value of `::path` alone; trail retires.


**Decision.** A raised error holds after the facts of its site its trace, `:trace`, a value of `::trace` whose elements are `::frame`s, a frame for each step that raised it or handed it on, from the step where it arose to the step where it is read; the verb `trace` answers it, `::trace[]` for an error that holds none, `"x" | add 1 !| keys` answering `#[:actualType :trace]`. An error at rest whose `:trace` holds a frame resumes it when raised, an empty `:trace` holds no frame, so its raise starts the trace at its own frame, and a `:trace` that is no vector of frames is refused as `::error/TraceNotFramesError`. A path is a value of `::path` alone: the path a read followed is the fact `:path`, and a frame holds the path of the part a verb's code broke on. The section of errors that states it is [QSERR#trace], its cases `core/test/conformance/QSERR/trace.jsonl`.


**Why.** The maintainer's answer binds (13:01), and [D351] records it: trace keeps one image with the frame, a stack trace of stack frames, the prior the corpus of other projects writes beside frame, where trail made of frames is a path of windows; in the files naming `::trail` the trail was called a trail and a path by turns, and trace, free in our texts, takes the one sense. One fact, one spelling: path keeps the one sense its kind gives it.


**Rationale.** The three deciders kept trail 3/3 and renamed only the path that meant it; none read the maintainer's answers of 12:56 and 13:01, which chose the trace once the session put the three roads to him with their cost, the trace everywhere, a field `:trail` over a kind `::trace`, or the trail kept. His answer binds; the deciders gave no argument for trail over trace, the measure finding trace free in our texts and frequent beside frame outside, so it does not outweigh his choice. Path for a value of `::path` alone is all three's and stays.


**Cost.** The fact `:trail`, the verb `trail`, the kind `::trail` with its source `core/lib/qlang/trail.qlang`, `::error/trail` and its refusals, and `::error/TrailNotFramesError` renamed for the trace, with their pages and laws (`core/lib/qlang/error.qlang`, `trail.qlang`, `frame.qlang`, `map.qlang`, `any.qlang`, the first page's chapter of errors and its laws, `cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang`); [QSERR] (its head, #trail renamed #trace with its file, #print, #rest, #raise, #catch) at the move, and every section whose cases print an error, each case claimed under the record in transit over its section; the alert of the command line and `core/src/elide.mjs`; `core/src/eval-trail.mjs` named for the trace, `core/src/runtime/raise.mjs` and `core/src/types.mjs`; the unit and integration tests that read a trail; the case names that say trail or path for it (`an-error-raised-again-resumes-its-path-at-its-last-frame`, `a-tagged-error-literal-whose-trail-is-no-path-is-refused-when-raised`, `trail-not-frames-in-literal`); the aim (“the error as a value with a trail”) and the texts and probes of [D344], [D339], [D327] and the other records in force that write the trail. The first page's screen changes, so `node scripts/benchmark.mjs check` runs before the commit. trail enters the lexicon [D350]. A query, a host or the sister project that reads `:trail` or `trail` breaks in one release.


**Removes.** Flagged, on the maintainer's words about this very axis: the word `trail`, the fact `:trail`, the kind `::trail` and its refusals, and the last name `TrailNotFramesError`, renamed for the trace; the section anchor [QSERR#trail], renamed [QSERR#trace].


**The maintainer's words.** «может поле называется и трейл но сам объект trace или как-то так.. первый вариант ты исходя из чего предлагал? просто что меньше переименований делать или как? .. » (maintainer, 2026-10-16 12:56, session d7f52e95); «выглядит так что на trace можно спокойно переехать .. по факту это просто переименование трейла ...  так что б оно с фреймом дружило лучше ..» (13:01), his answer to the question that put the trace everywhere, the field `:trail` beside a kind `::trace`, or the trail kept; «да, закладывай как вектор указывающий направление в котором следует двигаться тоже ..» (13:10); «а сам трейл почему не тэг? или это лишнее?..» (05:19), the kind of its own.

But «A path is a value of `::path` alone» is false after its list. The page of `doc` calls a vector of anchors a path: «a vector of anchors walks a path», «An anchor of the path `doc` reads», «a path of anchors is a vector» (core/lib/qlang/any.qlang:1077-1110). [QSSLF#axis] does too, «a vector of names as a path of members», and part 4 writes «a vector of anchors is a path of nodes». Path enters no lexicon, so nothing catches these. ## 23. Pattern and regex

Agreement 3/3, size M.

**Road.** Regex everywhere, the word of the kind, of `toRegex` and of the maintainer; pattern leaves; an input that takes a text as well as a regex is named for its role.


**Decision.** A regex is a value of the kind `::regex`, written `#"…"` or read from a string by `toRegex`. `match`, `capture` and `scan` take a regex alone, their input `:regex`, refusing a text as `::string/match/regex/KindNotTakenError`, while `contains`, `split` and `replace` take a regex or a text, told apart by its kind, `replace` naming what it seeks `:needle` as `contains` does. A string that writes no regex is refused by `toRegex` as `::string/toRegex/TextNotRegexError`, `get [~(#"a" | type) ~("(" | toRegex !| type) ~(::string/match | binding | get /value | inputs | map ~(get /name))]` answering `[::regex ::string/toRegex/TextNotRegexError [:regex]]`. Its chapter is Text and regexes, its section [QSTXT#regex].


**Why.** [D349]: regex has one exact sense, the word of the kind, of `toRegex`, of the code and of the maintainer, where pattern also names a pattern over a text, a glob and a pattern of design, and is far commoner in other projects. One fact, one spelling: an input named for a regex takes a regex alone, so `replace`, whose input takes `#[::regex ::string]`, is named for the role it shares with `contains`.


**Rationale.** All three chose regex everywhere and renamed the input of `replace` `:regex` with the others; but `replace` takes a regex or a text, `[::string/replace] | map ~(binding | get /value | params | map ~(get [/name /kinds]))` answering `[[[:pattern #[::regex ::string]] [:with #[::path ::quote ::string]]]]` and `"a.b" | replace "." "-"` answering `"a-b"`, so a slot named `:regex` would take a string; the decision names it `:needle`, the role `contains` gives the same slot, while `match`, `capture` and `scan` take `::regex` alone.


**Cost.** The inputs `:pattern` of `match`, `capture` and `scan` renamed `:regex` and that of `replace` `:needle`, with their refusals (`::string/match/pattern/KindNotTakenError` and its kin), pages and laws (`core/lib/qlang/string.qlang`); `::string/toRegex/TextNotPatternError` renamed `::string/toRegex/TextNotRegexError` with its page, its laws (`:TextNotPatternError/namesTheFlagsAsAPart` named for the input `flags`) and its constant; [QSTXT] (its title and head, #pattern renamed #regex with its file `core/test/conformance/QSTXT/pattern.jsonl` renamed `regex.jsonl`, #match, #search, #split), [QSORD#order] (pattern among the kinds) and `docs/spec/README.md` at the move; the first page (“A pattern, `#"…"`”, the law `:values/isToldByHowItsPrintOpens`) and `core/lib/qlang/regex.qlang`; `core/src/pattern.mjs` named for the regex it reads, with `patternProblem`, `patternRefusal`, `PATTERN_FLAGS` and the citations of [QSTXT#pattern] across `core/src` and in [D340]; the comments of `core/src/grammar.peggy`; the cases of QSTXT; this record's probe. `TextNotPatternError` enters the lexicon [D350]; pattern, a generic word, stays out of it. A query or the sister project that reads `…/pattern/…` or `TextNotPatternError` breaks in one release.


**Removes.** Flagged: the input name `:pattern`, renamed `:regex` and, for `replace`, `:needle`; the last name `TextNotPatternError`, renamed; the section anchor [QSTXT#pattern], renamed [QSTXT#regex].


**The maintainer's words.** «вложи там всю свою душу и талант в нормальную реализацию регекспов..» (maintainer, 2026-10-12 20:15, session 014e5a04); «может ещё языке есть где регексп это сущность первого рода» (20:22).

But pattern stays in chapters and pages its list leaves out, and pattern enters no lexicon: [QSLIT#kind] «`#"…"` the pattern» (:14), [QSLIT#atoms] (:214), [QSCND#compare] (:15), [QSPRN#json] (:54), [QSSYN#step] (:28), the page of `::keyword` «keywords rank after patterns» (keyword.qlang:19), the order on the page of `sort` (vec.qlang:986), and the pages of `split`, `contains`, `startsWith`, `endsWith` and `::string/toString` in string.qlang. ## 24. Body and recipe, and the scope of the code a verb runs stated twice

Agreement 2/3, size S.

**Road.** Recipe for the code a verb runs, body for the step of a declaration, the scope a call opens stated once in [QSBLD#recipe].


**Decision.** A recipe is the code a function runs, which `impl` gives it, or a quote written alone under a label; it runs in the scope its call opens, stated once in [QSBLD#recipe]: the root, the verbs, the nodes and the labels of the scope it is called in, `args` and each input by its name, and the names its steps declare, a value the reader bound to a bare name standing outside it. [QSNAM#scope] names that scope by citing [QSBLD#recipe] and holds the rules of declaring in it. The body of a declaration is the step after its name and its page, `:body` of `::bind`, run once where it stands, and a body that fails declares nothing.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: one scope, one statement. The order of concepts is the order of dependencies: the scope a call opens holds the args and the inputs, which [QSBLD#input] defines, so it is stated after them. Recipe is the maintainer's word for the code of a verb, and body keeps the one sense of the declaration's step, which [QSNAM#declare] gives it.


**Rationale.** Deciders 1 and 2 state the scope once in [QSBLD#recipe], decider 3 in [QSNAM#scope] by the order of the chapters; the scope rests on the args and the inputs that [QSBLD#input] defines, so the order of dependencies puts the statement there, and [QSNAM#scope] cites it forward as it already cites [QSNAM#owner].


**Cost.** [QSNAM#scope] (“The body of a word runs in the scope its call opens”), [QSNAM#verb] (“its body runs on the subject”), [QSNAM#owner] (“Inside the body of a word”), [QSQUO#held] (“The scope of a word's body”) and [QSBLD#recipe] at the move; the pages that say the body of a word (`core/lib/qlang/call.qlang` and their kin); `evalBody`, `bodyEnv` and `CALL_BINDS_KEY` of `core/src/eval.mjs` and `core/src/env-keys.mjs` named for the recipe and its scope, written with the evaluator over the steps [D339] part 1. No case.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «цитата участвует в рецепте глагола ! но это не глагол!» (maintainer, 2026-10-13 06:21, session d65e4c1d).


**Files.** In D:/git/qlang: docs/spec/QSNAM-names-and-scope.md, QSBLD-functions-and-kinds.md, QSQUO-code-as-data.md, core/lib/qlang/call.qlang and the pages that say the body of a word, core/src/eval.mjs, core/src/env-keys.mjs


**Checker: refuted.** Recipe for the code a verb runs, body for the step of a declaration, and the scope stated once in [QSBLD#recipe] all stand. But `evalBody` does not belong to the recipe. It evaluates every pipeline: the query (core/src/eval.mjs:63, 72), an invariant's condition (:597), a place (:630), a call's recipe (:808) and a group (:1162). Naming it for the recipe would misname four of its five uses, against [QSWRK#code].

**Probe.** grep -n -E 'evalBody[(]' core/src/eval.mjs → :63, :72, :160, :597, :630, :808, :1162; qlang ':n 3 | :number/times ~(mul n) | 5 | times !| type' → ::call/UnknownNameError

**Repair.** Name `bodyEnv` and `CALL_BINDS_KEY` for the recipe and its scope, and `evalBody` for the pipeline it runs, or leave it to the evaluator that D339 part 1 writes.


## 25. Expected, two things under one fact, and the rules of the parser among the facts

Agreement 2/3, size S.

**Road.** `:expected` kept on both refusals, each declared under its own refusal, the habit of every reader of a refusal; the parser's names for its rules give way to the words of the language.


**Decision.** `:expected` is what could have stood where a value or a text was refused, declared under each refusal that holds it: for a holder the set of the kinds it takes, `::error/expected`, `1 | add "x" !| get /expected` answering `#[::number]`; for a failure to parse the continuations the reading could take where it stopped, `::quote/ParseError/expected`, beside what it met as `:found`. ## The deciders' answers

The three answers the reconciler read, each through its lens, as each decider returned it.

### Through the mission

**Probes moved.** None. `node scripts/sensors/run-probes.mjs docs/journal/D341.md` answers ok for each of its probes on the tree 7f09ed8e (master, the branch the-open-roads-chosen at 0 ahead), so the tree has not moved under any part since the record's commit; the probes each decision changes move with its work, and the record holds the new answer of each beside the section it overrides until the move.

#### 1. What a call takes, by some nine words

**Road.** The maintainer's road: modifier for what a step writes, args for what its call computes, input for what a function declares, object, argument, param and part leaving (the record's first road with `params` becoming `inputs`).


**Decision.** A command is a verb and its modifiers: a modifier is what a step writes after its verb, `:modifiers` of `::call` holding each as the step it is, `~(add x) | first | get /modifiers` answering `[::call{:name :x}]`. The args are the values the modifiers computed against the subject, one value under the tag of the verb, `::number/add[1 2]`, an element of them an arg: a recipe reads them as `args`, `apply` and `fill` take them as `:args`, a call that gives more than its inputs read is refused as `…/ArgsBeyondInputsError`, and a fold calls its verb with the value so far as the subject and the element as its arg. An input is what a function declares by `takes`, its name, its page, the place of the args it reads, `/0` or `~(query /0 1)`, and its kinds, and `inputs` answers them, `::fn | takes :by |~~ b ~~| /0 ::number | impl ~(add by) | inputs | first | keys` answering `#[:kinds :name :page :place]`. The flags of `toRegex` are its modifier, `toRegex "i"`. A query starts from a subject, the data it is given, which the command line reads from standard input [QSHST#stdin]; object names a JSON object alone, and argument an argument of the command line's process alone.


**Why.** The maintainer's answer binds. It serves One fact, one spelling, the chapter already defining modifier, args and input apart [QSBLD#input], and Words carry paradigms: object, set aside once because a model trained on code reads it as the receiver of a call (D164), meets the JSON object of every input the mission names, and params beside inputs would name one concept twice. The first page loses its second sense of input, so the reader learns each word once.


**Cost.** Pages: `::fn` (object, arguments, params, the laws `:answersTheFailureOfAnObject`, `:SignatureNotCommandsError/refusesACommandWithNoObjects`, `:SignatureNotCommandsError/refusesAnObjectPastTheKinds`, `:call/refusesArgumentsPastItsInputs`, `:writesTheArguments`, the input pages of `fill`), `::kind`, `::any/apply`; the first page (“the input it is given”, “Look at the input”, “the element as the modifier”, “takes no object”, “where each of its objects came from”, the laws `:takesNoObjectForARecipeWrittenAlone`, `:looksAtTheInput`, `:looksAtTheShapeOfTheInput` renamed by their rule); the repair of `toRegex` (`REPAIRS` in `core/src/pattern.mjs`) and its law `:TextNotPatternError/namesTheFlagsAsAPart`; the head of `cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang`, `site/src/pages/index.astro`, `core/README.md`. Sections at the move: [QSBLD#fn] and #input, [QSQUO#apply] and #held, [QSCOL#reduce], [QSNUM#arithmetic] and #combine, [QSSLF#catalog], [QSHST#input] renamed #stdin with the citations of `cli/src` and `core/src/walk.mjs`. The word `::fn/params` renamed `::fn/inputs` with its laws and its case of QSBLD/fn. Code: `objectsOf` of `core/src/runtime/signature.mjs`, `codeOfObject` and `refusalOfFailedObject` of `core/src/eval.mjs`, `selector` and `selectorOfPlace` of `core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs` named modifier, arg and place. D276 decided by this part; D272's words. A query or the sister project that calls `params` breaks in one release.


**Removes.** The word `params` (`::fn/params`), renamed `inputs`, on the maintainer's answer; no capability, form or shape of a value.


**The maintainer's words.** «modifier / input / args (Рекомендую)» (maintainer, 2026-10-16 12:21 UTC, session d7f52e95), the option that has a step write modifiers, a function declare inputs (params→inputs), a call's value be its args, and object, argument, param and part leave; «svoObject тогда как-то наверное лучше .. что б усилить сигнал.. и не мешать с ооп.. такое допустимо? ..» and «ок, давай args» (2026-10-09 18:02 and 18:03, 22b934f8); «::Box/put[10]  итого это у нас раскладывается на  ::Box - S (subject), put - V (verb), а vec [10] - это O (object)» (2026-10-09 03:33, 22b934f8); «а то что у нас в проекте не было и даже порицилось использование всякой классической объектно-ориентированной замусоренной терминологии в виде класса и объекта и т.п..» (2026-09-23 08:28, 86982eb5).
