#### 2. The role of a tag that names what lies under it: label, noun, kind, tag and topic

**Road.** The record's first road: the defined terms kept, one definition of a label written in [QSNAM#address] and cited by [QSBLD#label], noun the head of any address, topic the name of a label and never a third kind of name.


**Decision.** A tag names a kind and an address, `::Box`. A label is a kind declared under a name, by the chain that builds it from `::kind` or by its page alone, which declares a kind of no commands: its verbs, refusals and pages lie under its address, and `kinds` answers the labels the scope reads, `:Box |~~ B ~~| | kinds | has ::Box` answering `true` [QSNAM#address]. A noun is the name an address names before its last name, whatever it holds: a label, `::Box` in `::Box/area`, a verb, `::number/add` in `::number/add/addend`, or a name of data, `:x` in `:x/y`; a name declared under no noun is refused as `::bind/NounNotDeclaredError`. The topic of the language is the label `::qlang`, and the topic of the command line `::cli`, the label whose page opens the pages of its host; a label is reached by its tag, `::string`, `::qlang`. What `tells` declares under a topic is a chapter, the page of a concept, and no kind.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms and One fact, one spelling: label is the maintainer's word, the first page teaches it, and its two definitions become one; folding label and noun into kind and tag leaves the noun of a verb and of data with no term, the critique's counterexample; topic listed beside label and kind made one thing three.


**Cost.** [QSNAM#address] holds the one definition, [QSBLD#label] and the head of [QSBLD] cite it; [QSNAM#reach] “A label, a kind or a topic, is reached by its tag” said of a label; [QSSLF#page]; the first page's chapter of verbs (“A kind is a label built from `::kind`…”); the page of `kinds` (“A concept of the topic is no kind”, the law `:holdsNoConcept`, said of a chapter); the comments of `core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs` that list a topic beside a kind. No case, no refusal, no word of the surface.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «да, допиши потому что это важно .. и добавь в наш словарь терминов "легенда", "легенда полезной нагрузки" "ярлык"» (maintainer, 2026-10-08 23:30, session 22b934f8); «::Box - это не объект со скрытой структурой, это ты верно заметил .. ярлык на данных.. разметка данных» (2026-10-08 21:27, 22b934f8).

#### 3. Verb and word

**Road.** The maintainer's road: verb everywhere.


**Decision.** A verb is a function named under a label, which a command calls by its name, `::vec/sort` the `sort` of vectors. Each step is a command, a verb and what it takes; the verb is found through the kind of the subject, and the chapter `::qlang/verbs` tells the verbs that live on kinds. The chapters, the first page, the pages, the refusals, the facts, the code, the cases and the tools say verb: `get [~(5 | count !| type) ~(:count 5 | [1 2] | count !| type)]` answers `[::call/KindAnswersNoVerbError ::call/ValueMeetsVerbError]`; the core exports `verbNames`, `verbsReaching` and `isVerb`, and `tokenize` takes the names of the verbs as `verbNames` and paints a call that reaches one as a `verb`. Word is no term of the language: it keeps its plain sense, a word of a text or of a source, `true`, `false` and `null` the words of the literals, where no reader takes it for a verb.


**Why.** The maintainer's answer binds, and his principle of words: verb has one exact sense, the V of the subject–verb–object frame he named, where word names a function, a word of text, a token of the source and the name of a literal at once (the critique's counterexample).


**Cost.** Every chapter of the language says verb (the heads of [QSTXT] and [QSCOL], the title of [QSDSP] and its file name `docs/spec/QSDSP-a-word-found-through-its-subject.md`, [QSHST#repl] on the class of a call), `docs/spec/README.md`, the first page and its link text, every page of the catalog and `cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang`; the refusal `::call/ValueMeetsWordError` renamed `::call/ValueMeetsVerbError` with its page, its laws and its cases; the law keys that say word (`:isAWordNamedUnderALabel`, `:names/meetsAWordAloud`, `:quotesOneWord`, `:verbs/refusesTheTagForAWordOfValues` and some forty more); the case names that say word, under this record over their sections; the export `isWord`, the class `'word'` of `core/src/highlight.mjs` and its option `builtinNames`, the painters of the command line, the language server, the site and the editor; the names and comments of the core; the seeds (`CLAUDE.md`, the READMEs) and the aim where word means a verb or a name. The benchmark's tasks already say verb. The completion kind the language server hands the editor stays the editor's enum.


**Removes.** The last name `ValueMeetsWordError` and the export `isWord`, renamed, on the maintainer's answer; no capability.

#### 4. Member and node

**Road.** The record's road with part 3's word: node for a place of the tree of names, verb for the value that runs, anchor for the name an axis reads, no member; the parser's node leaves with the evaluator over the steps.


**Decision.** The names form one tree, every node a binding, and what lies under a name is its nodes, which `manifest` answers. An axis given an anchor, a keyword, reads the node the subject holds under it, an input of a verb or the verb a name calls on a noun, `::map | doc :minus` the page of `::map/minus`, and a vector of anchors is a path of nodes; the chapters of a topic are its nodes. The value of a verb, a recipe or a function whose recipe is one, is what runs on the values of its label (`isVerbValue`, `callVerbOn`). Node names no step: the parser reads a source into the records of its steps and their spans [D339], and a vector or a set holds an element or lacks it, no membership said.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms: member is a word of objects and classes the maintainer asked the texts to keep away from; One fact, one spelling: node is already the word of the first page and of [QSNAM#address].

**Road.** The record's road taken as chain: signature leaves, and the refusal is named for the chain it refuses, what it refuses staying D272's.

#### 6. A host's implementation and the host

**Road.** A road of my own beside the record's: host for every tool that hands the language its sources, the core first, primitive for the JavaScript a host hands and hand for handing it; implementation, kernel and the hosts that meant the engine and the terminal leave, the core's words staying a host's.


**Decision.** A host is a tool that hands the language its sources, each `{ uri, source, primitives }`: the core is the first host, of the sources of the language, and the command line, the site and the sister project are hosts, so a verb of the core and a verb of the command line answer alike, each a `::builtin{}` [QSNAM#owner]. `impl` gives a function its recipe, code or `::builtin{}`, which stands for a primitive, the JavaScript function the host hands for that address beside its source, and the runtime, the evaluator with the table of the primitives handed and the values it reads, `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`, runs it. A `::builtin{}` the host hands no primitive for, a primitive handed for a name no `::builtin{}` declares and one handed twice are refused as `::builtin/PrimitiveNotHandedError`, `::builtin/PrimitiveNamesNoBuiltinError` and `::builtin/PrimitiveHandedTwiceError`, and the platform a loader ran on is the `:platform` of `::builtin/SourceLoadError`. The engine bounds the length of a vector and reads a regex alike on every platform, and the terminal hands a paste in chunks.


**Why.** One model: the core and a host are one mechanism, so one name, the critique's conflict with the record's road of naming the core's words the runtime's; One fact, one spelling: primitive is the word of the pages, the chapters and the table, implementation and kernel named the same things again, and bind is the language's word for a declaration.


**Cost.** [QSHST#source] (`impls`, the kernel, binds), [QSHST#repl] (the terminal), [QSERR#site] (an invariant of the runtime); the pages of `::builtin` (its head, three refusals, `:host`), `::number/range` (the length), `::regex` (the engine), `::call/HostFailureError` (a host's primitive); the case `an-invariant-of-the-kernel-sits-under-builtin` of QSERR/site renamed; `bindPrim`, `bindStateReader` and `PRIMITIVE_REGISTRY` of `core/src/primitives.mjs` and every module of `core/src/runtime/` that hands its primitives, `HOST_IMPLS`, `hostImplOf` and `declareHostValue` of `core/src/types.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/root.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/bootstrap.mjs`, `core/src/source-load-error.mjs`, `core/src/session.mjs`, `cli/src/cli-sources.mjs`, `cli/src/line-editor.mjs`, `core/README.md`; the host's field `impls`, which the sister project's sources hand and D293 names, renamed `primitives`.


**Removes.** The host's field `impls`, renamed `primitives`; the last names of three refusals of `::builtin` and the fact `:host` of a loader's refusal, renamed; no capability.

#### 8. Shape, and outline

**Road.** A road of my own: shape names the structure of a value, which `shape` answers and a template writes, outline what `paths` answers, schema the gloss that defines a shape, and the uses that meant the kind of the core or a value the runtime reads say so.


**Decision.** The shape of a value is its structure, the kinds of its parts, the keys of its maps and the shape of its elements: `shape` answers it under `::shape`, the schema of the value in its own literal, `[1] | shape` answering `::shape[::number]`; a template of `get` writes the shape of an answer with a path or a quote in each place, `get {:who /first}`; and a task that asks an answer of a shape asks the same thing. The outline of a value is what `paths` answers, the set of its leaves, each the path that reads it and its kind. The walk of a subject's tags ends at the kind of the core beneath them, and the runtime reads the values `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling and Words carry paradigms: the shape `shape` answers, the shape a template builds and the shape a benchmark's task asks are one thing, which the maintainer named `shape` and `::shape`; the few uses that meant the kind of the core or a value the runtime reads were the other things, and outline keeps the one word of `paths`.


**Cost.** The first page's chapter of verbs (“then the shape beneath them”), [QSHST#source] (“the shapes it reads”), a comment of `core/src/grammar.peggy`, `shapeKindOf` of `core/src/ordering.mjs` named for what it answers; the aim (“the outline of a value”, “its outline”) and the title and text of D327 say shape where they mean what `shape` answers. No case, no word of the surface.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «просто тут вопрос .. шейп это ответ глагола и шейп конкретного литерала .. может оно разное ... понимаешь?..» (maintainer, 2026-10-15 16:32, session 70702c43); «ок почему тогда сам shape не отвечает каким-то ::shape ?..» (17:09, 70702c43); «ты хотели предоставить модели какой-то аутлайн произвольного значения» (21:01, 70702c43), of what `shape` gives, which the language calls a shape; «оглавление - бесплатная выведенная схема для всего» (2026-10-13 01:24, d65e4c1d), of the paths of a value.

#### 9. Env and scope

**Road.** A road of my own from the record's frame: scope for the names a step sees and the steps that share them, env for the word alone, the code and the package naming the scope as the texts do.


**Decision.** A scope is the steps of one pipeline and the names they see over the root: its first step and every step `|` or `!|` joins to it, a group and each run of a quote a scope of its own, and the scope a call opens [QSBLD#recipe]. `env` answers the declarations of the reader in the scope where its step stands, the root apart, `:x 1 | env | keys` answering `#[:x]`. The state of a step is its value and its scope, `state.scope`, a session keeps the scope its cells write, `createSession({ scope })` and `session.scope`, and the environment of the command line's process is the host's `process.env`.


**Why.** [QSWRK#code] and One fact, one spelling: the texts a reader reads say scope, and the aim's core is a state pair of a value and a scope; the word `env` keeps its one sense, the reader's declarations, which a reader would take for the whole scope were the scope an env.


**Cost.** The names of the scope in the core, the command line, the language server and the site (`state.env`, `envSet`, `withEnv`, `envHas`, `envAfterCell`, `core/src/env-keys.mjs` renamed `scope-keys.mjs`), riding with the evaluator over the steps of [D339]; `core/README.md` (“`env` is the current scope”), the comment of `langRuntime` in `core/src/runtime/index.mjs` (the root, the scope every query starts from), [QSNAM#scope] said without env; the session's option and property, which D293 names among what a host builds on, so a host that hands or reads a session's `env` breaks in one release. No case.


**Removes.** The session's `env` option and property, renamed `scope`.


**The maintainer's words.** «или для этого env и есть в паре (pipeValue, env)» (maintainer, 2026-09-28 06:09, session d427ce7e) and «литерал это форма которая не зависит ни от env ни от pipeValue» (2026-10-13 19:07, d65e4c1d): his words name the code's pair, no term he chose, which the aim names a value and a scope.

#### 10. Frame

**Road.** The text as it stands.


**Decision.** A frame is one level of a stack of calls: a step, a call, a modifier its call computes, a literal, a group and each run of the code a verb runs nest a frame one below their own, the budget counting them and refusing a runaway as `::call/EvaluationDepthExceededError`, and the trail of a failure holds a frame for each level it left, `::frame[subject steps place]`. The sources of a host run in a scope over the root, and a frame of the terminal's output is the line editor's, beyond the boundary.


**Why.** One model: the depth and the path of a failure are counted in one kind of level, the paradigm of a stack of frames; the one sense apart, a frame over the root, is already a scope in `core/src/runtime/root.mjs`.


**Cost.** None.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «и как бы у на каждом уровне есть свой субъект и свой трейл..» (maintainer, 2026-09-26 06:26, session 86982eb5).


Size S.

**Files.** None.


#### 11. Source, uri and origin

**Road.** A road of my own: source for code as written, origin, a keyword, for where a text came from on every value that names it, `:uri` leaving the facts.


**Decision.** A source is code as written: the text a host hands as `{ uri, source, primitives }`, the step that declared a binding under its `:source`, every declaration under an address, which the axis `source` reads, and the line where a parse stopped under the `:source` of `::quote/ParseError` where [D339] keeps it. Where a text came from is its `:origin`, a keyword, the source, the cell or the verb that read it, on a binding, a failure to parse and a refusal of the root alike, `get [~("add (" | parse !| get /origin) ~(::vec/count | binding | get /origin)]` answering `[:string/parse :qlang/vec]`; the root reads the `uri` a host hands into it.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: one fact, one name and one kind for where a text came from, which a binding names by `:origin`, a keyword, and a failure by `:uri`, a string.


**Cost.** The fact `:ParseError/uri` of `::quote/ParseError` and the `:uri` of the refusals of the root renamed `:origin` with their pages (`core/lib/qlang/quote.qlang`, `core/lib/qlang/builtin.qlang`), [QSSYN#refusal], [QSQUO#parse], the case of QSSYN/refusal and the unit test that read it, `core/src/error-convert.mjs`, `core/src/parse.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/root.mjs`; the host's field `uri` stays.


**Removes.** The fact `:uri`, renamed `:origin`.


**The maintainer's words.** None.

#### 12. Parse, both ways

**Road.** A road of my own: `parse` reads text alone, and the text of a quote is its `content`, as a doc's is, `::quote/parse` renamed and `toString` refused on a quote as on every container.


**Decision.** `parse` reads text into the quote it writes, `"add 1" | parse` answering `~(add 1)`, a source that reads as no pipeline refused as `::quote/ParseError`. The text of a quote is its `content`, as the text of a doc is, the steps printed in one spelling, `~(add 1 | mul 2) | content` answering `"add 1 | mul 2"` and `"5 |  mul   2" | parse | content` answering `"5 | mul 2"`; `print` writes its literal and `json` the JSON of that literal. `toString` turns an atom and a name into its text and refuses a quote as it refuses a container, naming the verbs that do the act, `~(add 1) | get [~(content) ~(print) ~(json) ~(toString !| get /nearest)]` answering `["add 1" "~(add 1)" "\"~(add 1)\"" [:content :json :print]]`. `parseJson` reads JSON and `json` writes it.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms: parse reads text, and one name that reads text on a string and writes it on a quote turns the habit around; content already answers the text a doc holds between its brackets; the maintainer's plan has `toString` refuse a container naming the verbs that do its act (D244, D247).


**Cost.** The verb `::quote/parse` renamed `::quote/content` with its page and laws (`core/lib/qlang/quote.qlang`), the page of `::string/toString` that links it, the first page's law `:errors/holdsTheSkippedSteps` (`skipped | parse` read as `skipped | content`), the laws of `source` in `core/lib/qlang/any.qlang` and the pages of `::trail` and `::frame` that read a quote's text by `parse`, [QSQUO#parse] and its cases, the probe of [QSHST#source]; the sister project's queries that call `parse` on a quote.


**Removes.** The verb `::quote/parse`, renamed `content`; reading text by `parse` stays.


**The maintainer's words.** «дистинкт может оставь, как запасной ход-синоним для прайоров высаживающихся на него..  а так да, записывай и погнали делать..» (maintainer, 2026-10-12 19:18, session 014e5a04), the plan of D244, whose refusal of `toString` on a container names `print` and `json` (D247).

#### 13. The refusal of what a holder takes is named two ways

**Road.** The record's road for the records of the steps, the two refusals of a subject and of a holder kept as the two rules of [QSERR#site] the critique showed them to be.


**Decision.** A value of none of the kinds a holder takes is refused by the holder's role, its kinds the fact `:expected`: an input, an answer, a payload, an element, the subject of a function `apply` runs, `…/SubjectKindNotTakenError`, and the payload of a record of a step, `::call`, `::bind`, `::construct`, `::fail` and `::group` alike, `…/PayloadKindNotTakenError`, `get [~(::call[1] !| get [~(type) /expected]) ~(::fail[1] !| get [~(type) /expected])]` answering `[[::call/PayloadKindNotTakenError #[::map]] [::fail/PayloadKindNotTakenError #[::quote]]]`. A map whose fields break the schema of its record is refused as `…/PayloadNotSchemaError`, its `:field` the field. The subject of a verb is refused by the label it resides on, `…/SubjectNot<Label>Error`, with no `:expected`, `"x" | add 1 !| type` answering `::number/add/SubjectNotNumberError`.


**Why.** [QSERR#site]: residence and the kinds of a holder are two rules with facts of their own, so two names; One fact, one spelling: a payload of another kind is one refusal on every record of a step, where `:field` null named nothing.


**Cost.** The refusal of a payload that is no map under `::call`, `::bind` and `::construct` (`recordConstructor` in `core/src/runtime/quote-steps.mjs`), the pages of `call.qlang`, `bind.qlang` and `construct.qlang` and the case of QSQUO/step; [QSQUO#step] at the move. With [D339] part 1 the schema of the steps is what makes data a quote.


**Removes.** None.

#### 14. Place, index and key

**Road.** A road of my own: index for the number of an element, key for what a map reads, place for a path that locates a part, segment for what a path read where the refusal names no key and no index.


**Decision.** A path reads at each segment a key of a map or an index of a vector, from zero, a negative index counted from the end, and `segments` answers the keys and the indices of a path. A refusal of a path names the index it read, `::path/IndexOutOfBoundsError` with `:index` and `:length`, the key a map lacks, `::path/KeyNotInMapError` with `:key`, and otherwise the segment it read, `::path/IndexNotIntegerError` and `::path/SubjectHasNoPartsError` with `:segment`, `[10 20] | get /name !| get [~(type) /segment]` answering `[::path/IndexNotIntegerError :name]`. A place is a path that locates a part: the place a frame names of the element a verb's code broke on, `trail | last | place` answering `/1`, the place of the args an input reads, `/0` or `~(query /0 1)`, and the place of the payload a field reads. Vectors of one length whose elements part index by index are tuples.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: the prose said place where the facts and the refusals say index, and `:key` held a segment that is no key; place keeps the one sense the verbs `place` and `takes :place` give it.


**Cost.** The pages of `::path` and `::vec` and of `segments` (“a place of a vector”, “`:index` holds the place read”), [QSPTH#value], #get and #words, [QSSLF#axis] (“the place in the set”), the law `:readsTuplesPlaceByPlace`; the facts `:IndexNotIntegerError/key` and `:SubjectHasNoPartsError/key` renamed `:segment` with their pages, their two laws and the case that reads one; `core/src/path-read.mjs`.


**Removes.** The fact `:key` of two refusals of a path, renamed `:segment`.


**The maintainer's words.** None.

#### 15. Marker, mark, absent, each and address

**Road.** A road of my own: marker for the value of `::elision`, caret for the line under a failure to parse, mark for the tags of a shape, absent for `::absent` alone, `::each` kept and its section renamed, email for the key a shape folds.


**Decision.** A part a view left out gives way to a marker, a value of `::elision`, whose `:read` continues the query to it. A failure to parse draws its `:caret` under its `:source`, where [D339] keeps a caret among the facts. The tags of a shape over a part are its marks, `::absent` over a key some records lack and `::nullable` over a part null in some; `::null` is the kind of null, the value that stands for nothing. A map whose keys are data folds into `::each` over its first key, a key that reads as an id, a date, a time, a uuid or an email among them. The section of [QSCOL] that tells the code `map` runs on each element is [QSCOL#mapping].


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: marker is the maintainer's word for the elision and the word of its pages, so the line under a source takes the word of what it draws; absent and address each had a second sense; `::each` is the maintainer's word, so the section moves.

#### 16. Anchor and name, right and left

**Road.** The record's road with the words of the language: `:anchor` the name every axis takes, `:condition` the boolean `and` and `or` take, right and left leaving.


**Decision.** An axis that takes the name of a node takes it as `:anchor`, a keyword, `binding`, `doc`, `runLaws` and `source` alike, `::vec/take | get [~(source 1 !| type) ~(binding 1 !| type)]` answering `[::any/source/anchor/KindNotTakenError ::any/binding/anchor/KindNotTakenError]`; which axes take one is [D294]'s. `and` and `or` join the subject and the condition they take, `true | and (5 | gt 3)`, a condition of another kind refused as `::boolean/and/condition/KindNotTakenError`; a comparison takes the `:value` it compares with and the algebra the `:other` set or map.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: one input, one name across the axes; Words carry paradigms: right and left are the words of an infix operator where a step has a subject and what its verb takes, and a condition is the language's word for a boolean a verb reads.

#### 17. The kinds of the steps

**Road.** A road of my own: `::invariant` the kind of an invariant, as `::law` is the kind of a law, the records of the steps kept with the sentences that define them.


**Decision.** An invariant is a query the values of a kind or of an input keep, a quote under `::invariant`, which `holds` writes in a chain, as a law is a quote under `::law`, which `proves` writes: `::number/div/divisor/ZeroError | binding | get /value | type` answers `::invariant`. A step is stored as the record of its form [QSQUO#step]: a call as `::call`, a declaration as `::bind`, which writes a `::binding` into its scope, a tagged literal as `::construct`, whose `:payload` is the payload the literal writes and whose constructor is `::construct/construct` as every label's is `construct`, a step of the fail track as `::fail` and a group as `::group`, `~(:x 1 | ::Box[1] !| 0) | map ~type` answering `[::bind ::construct ::fail]`.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms: a tag names a kind, and an invariant is a thing, not the act of its command; the maintainer asked for `::invariant`, and `::holds` was kept on a choice he left to the model; the records of the steps each have a sentence that defines them.


**Cost.** The tag `::holds` renamed `::invariant`: its source `core/lib/qlang/holds.qlang` renamed, the first page's chapter of verbs (`#[::any ::fn ::holds ::kind]`), [QSBLD#label] and #chain (`:Positive ::holds~(gt 0)`), `INVARIANT_TAG` of `core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/bootstrap.mjs`, the cases that write `::holds`; data a reader wrote under `::holds` reads anew.


**Removes.** The tag `::holds`, renamed `::invariant`.

**Road.** A road of my own: one name, a pipeline query language, wherever qlang says what it is.

#### 22. The path of a failure

**Road.** The record's road: trail for what a failure flew, path for a value of `::path` alone.


**Decision.** A raised error holds after the facts of its site its trail, `:trail`, a value of `::trail`, a frame for each step that raised it or handed it on, from the step where it arose to the step where it is read; an error at rest that holds a trail resumes it when raised, and an empty `:trail` holds no frame, as an error holds none, so its raise starts the trail at its own frame. The verb `trail` answers the trail, `::trail[]` for an error that holds none. A path is a value of `::path`: the path a read followed is the fact `:path`, and a frame holds the path of the part a verb's code broke on.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: the trail is one thing with one word, the maintainer's, and path keeps the one sense its kind gives it.

#### 23. Pattern and regex

**Road.** A road of my own the maintainer's words and the kind give: regex everywhere.


**Decision.** A regex is a value of the kind `::regex`, written `#"\d+ms"` and read from a string by `toRegex`: `contains`, `split` and `replace` tell a regex from a text by its kind, and `match`, `capture` and `scan` take a regex alone, their input `:regex`, refusing a text as `::string/match/regex/KindNotTakenError`; a string that writes no regex is refused by `toRegex` as `::string/toRegex/TextNotRegexError`, `get [~(#"a" | type) ~("(" | toRegex !| type) ~(::string/match | binding | get /value | inputs | map ~(get /name))]` answering `[::regex ::string/toRegex/TextNotRegexError [:regex]]`. Its chapter is Text and regexes, its section [QSTXT#regex].


**Why.** The maintainer's principle of words: regex has one exact sense where pattern names many, and it is the word of the kind, of `toRegex` and of the maintainer; One fact, one spelling.

**Road.** The record's road: recipe the code a function runs, body a declaration's, the scope a call opens stated once in [QSBLD#recipe].

**Road.** The record's road: `:expected` the kinds alone, a failure to parse naming what could follow as `:next`, the parts of the grammar described in the words of the language.


**Decision.** `:expected` is the set of the kinds a holder takes, where a value of none of them was refused, `1 | add "x" !| get /expected` answering `#[::number]`. A failure to parse names what could follow where it stopped as `:next`, a string for a literal and a keyword for a part of the grammar, and what it met as `:found`, each part described as the language says it, a name, a number, whitespace, a doc, `":" | parse !| get /next` answering `["\"" :name]`. #### 32. Plain, over three things

**Road.** A road of my own: plain for a value under no tag alone, a primitive that reads no state said as taking the values alone, the objects of JavaScript named so and the exports that cross the boundary of JSON named for it.

#### 1. What a call takes, by some nine words

**Road.** Three words for the three things the tree tells apart, modifier, arg and input, with place for what an input reads; object leaves, as the maintainer's choice of args set it aside (D173); `params` becomes `inputs`; D276 is answered whole.


**Decision.** A modifier is what a command writes after its name, one word, held as the step it is under `:modifiers` of `::call`: `~(add x) | first | get /modifiers` answers `[::call{:name :x}]`. An arg is the value a modifier computes against the subject. The args of a call are the vector of them under the tag of its verb, `::number/add[1 2]`, which a recipe reads as `args`, `apply` and `fill` take, `apply f [3]`, and `…/ArgsBeyondInputsError` refuses past the places the inputs read. The element a fold hands its verb is its first arg. An input is what a function declares by `takes`: a name, a page, a place and kinds, `::number/add/addend`. `inputs` answers the inputs of a function: `::fn | takes :by |~~ b ~~| /0 ::number | impl ~(add by) | inputs | first | keys` answers `#[:kinds :name :page :place]`. The place of an input is what it reads of the args, a path or code over them, and the number of the arg it reads is an index. The flags of `toRegex` are its input `:flags`. A query starts from its subject, the value it is given. Object is the word of JSON alone.


**Why.** One name for one thing ([QSWRK#code]; the aim's One fact, one spelling). Words carry paradigms: a model trained on code reads object as the receiver of a call, our subject, which is why D173 set it aside. Arg and input are the pair the habit knows, and input is the word the surface already holds, in `ArgsBeyondInputsError`, `takes` an input, [QSBLD#input] and `:verbs/declaresTheInputs`, so this road renames one word where param everywhere would rename 186 refusals. It closes the critic's hole: three things, three words. [QSWRK#catalog] lets a name of habit such as `params` enter beside the verb where a task asks for one, and none does.


**Cost.** `::fn/params` becomes `::fn/inputs`, with its refusal `::fn/params/SubjectNotFnError`, its laws and the case `params-answers-the-inputs-of-a-function` (QSBLD/fn). Prose: [QSBLD#fn] (`apply` hands … arguments, `fill`, `params`), [QSBLD#input] (`/0/by` a key of an object), [QSNUM#arithmetic] (one number as their object, more objects), [QSCOL#reduce] (a call with one object, its first object), [QSSLF#catalog] (its objects → its modifiers), [QSQUO#held] (an object of a call → a modifier of a call). The first page: “the input it is given”, “the element as the modifier”, “a word and what it takes”. The pages of `::fn` (“`/0` the first object”, “as a call holds its objects”), `::kind`, `apply`, `fill`, the `reduce` of vec, set and map, and `::regex`. `REPAIRS` in `core/src/pattern.mjs` (the part of `toRegex` → its input). The law `:TextNotPatternError/namesTheFlagsAsAPart` becomes `…/namesTheFlagsAsAnInput`, under part 23's name. `core/README.md`, the head of `cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang` and the index of the site. In the code, `objectsOf` → `modifiersOf`, `selectorOfPlace`/`.selector` → the reading of a place, and `.place`, a number, → `.index`. D276 leaves as replaced. D272's text says args. The sister project renames `params` wherever it reads it.


**Removes.** The verb name `params`, renamed `inputs`; flagged as a removal, reason one name for one thing. No capability leaves.

#### 2. Label, noun, kind, tag and topic

**Road.** The defined terms kept, the record's first road, each use repaired to its term and the label defined once at [QSNAM#address]; folding label, noun and topic into kind and tag is set aside, since the noun of a verb or of data would be left with no term.


**Decision.** A tag names a kind, `::Box`, and is a value of its own. A kind is what a value is, the tag `type` answers; a tag no declaration answers names a kind of identity. A label is a kind declared under a name, either by its page alone, which makes the kind of no commands, or by the chain of `::kind`. Its verbs reside on it, and its tag lays itself over values through its constructor. A noun is the head of an address, the node a name with a path is declared under, whatever that node holds: a label, a verb (`::number/add` is the noun of `::number/add/addend`) or data (`:x` is the noun of `:x/y`). `::bind/NounNotDeclaredError` refuses a name whose noun stands nowhere. The topic of the language is the label `::qlang`: its page is the first page, and its nodes are its chapters, each the page of a concept. A label is reached by its tag.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: the two definitions of a label are written once. The order of concepts is the order of dependencies: [QSNAM#address] defines the label, and [QSBLD#label] rests on it with what a declared kind adds. The critic's counterexample (`::number/add/addend`, `:x/y`) keeps noun as its own term.


**Cost.** [QSNAM#address]; [QSNAM#reach], where “A label, a kind or a topic, is reached by its tag” becomes “A label is reached by its tag”; the head of [QSBLD], where “a label is a kind” becomes a label declared under a name; [QSBLD#label], which rests on [QSNAM#address]; [QSSLF#page], where “its members are its chapters, the concepts” becomes its nodes; the first page's verbs chapter, where “A verb lives on a kind” and “named under a label” become one sentence; and the index of the site, “A kind is a label declared by its page”. No case and no word of the surface changes.


**Removes.** none

#### 3. Verb and word

**Road.** Verb everywhere for a function named under a label, the V of subject, verb and args; word keeps one sense, a unit of written text.


**Decision.** A verb is a function named under a label, the label of the values it serves, `::vec/sort`. The verbs of a kind and those of every value are the whole of them: `manifest` lists them, `::qlang/verbs` tells them, and `verbNames` and `verbsReaching` answer them for a host. A value the scope binds meets a verb of its name aloud, `::call/ValueMeetsVerbError`, beside `::call/KindAnswersNoVerbError`. A word is a unit of the text a parser reads. A command is a name and its modifiers, each modifier one word; `true`, `false` and `null` are the words of the literals; and a string holds words, as in the law of `contains`, “A word of the string is found.”. The painters call the name of a call that reaches a verb a `verb`.


**Why.** The maintainer's principle, given in answer to this very question: verb has one exact sense, while word names four things today (a function, a token, the word of a literal, a word of a string). One name for one thing. Words carry paradigms: the maintainer says глагол throughout, and the aim says “few verbs over many addressable nouns”. The surface already says verb in `KindAnswersNoVerbError`, `verbNames`, `verbsReaching`, `::qlang/verbs`, the keys `:findsTheVerbOfTheKind`… and the benchmark's tasks (“the pages of the verbs”). So this road leaves every item of the critic's cost list as it stands, and leaves fewer things behind than word everywhere would.


**Cost.** `::call/ValueMeetsWordError` becomes `::call/ValueMeetsVerbError`: 46 occurrences in 20 files, with its page, laws and cases. The law keys that say Word for a verb (`:names/meetsAWordAloud`, `:readsThePageOfAWord` beside `:readsThePageOfAVerb`, …). The case names that say word for a verb. The prose of every chapter that does so: the chapter QSDSP is retitled “A verb found through its subject”, its file renamed and the 19 files that link it rewritten. Also [QSWRK#catalog] (“A word enters the catalog”), [QSHST#repl] (a `word`), the pages of the catalog and the first page. In the code, `isWord`, `nearestWords`, `applyWord` and kin, the painter's token `'word'` → `'verb'` (`core/src/highlight.mjs`, `site/src/lib/qlang-highlight.js`, the ANSI painter) and the hosts' `builtinNames` → `verbNames`. Where they say word for a verb, `CLAUDE.md` and the aim follow at the move. The sister project renames `ValueMeetsWordError` and the token where it reads them. `node scripts/benchmark.mjs check` runs before the commit.


**Removes.** The refusal tag `::call/ValueMeetsWordError` and the painter's token `word`, both renamed; flagged, reason one name for one thing and the maintainer's principle. No capability leaves.

#### 4. Member and node

**Road.** Node for a name under a noun and verb for the value that runs; member leaves, as the abstraction of objects the maintainer steered away from; the parser's nodes are named by their tree.


**Decision.** A node is a name under a noun in the tree of names, every node a binding. An axis given an anchor reads the node the subject holds under that name, an input of a verb or the verb a name calls on a noun: `::map | doc :minus`. The chapters of the topic are its nodes, and a node of a noun is read through its noun or at its address. The value that runs under a label is a verb's. The parser's tree has nodes of its own, named as the nodes of the parser's tree. The `:node` of the fact `:host` is the proper name of a platform.


**Why.** One name for one thing. The first page already says node (“a node under the noun”), and the tree of names is defined by it ([QSNAM#address]). Words carry paradigms: member wakes the members of a class, the terminology the maintainer had the project keep away from. The parser's tree is qualified wherever it is named, so the critic's hole is answered.


**Cost.** The pages of the axes in `core/lib/qlang/any.qlang` (`binding`, `doc`, `runLaws`, `laws`, `explain`, `tutorial`), [QSSLF#axis], [QSSLF#near] and [QSSLF#page]. In the code, `memberRecordOf` → the record of a node under the subject and `nearestMembers` → the nodes near a name (`core/src/runtime/axis.mjs`, `core/src/nearest-names.mjs`), `isMemberValue` → `isVerbValue` and `callMemberOn` → `callVerbOn` (`core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs`, `core/src/eval.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/manifest.mjs`). D294's text (“the anchor of a member”) follows, while which axes take an anchor stays D294's. No refusal or case changes.

#### 5. Chain, signature and commands

**Road.** Chain is the one word for the commands that build a function or a kind, and signature leaves. The refusal is named for what it refuses, a command its noun does not take, and a pair whose first is no quote goes to the refusal of the pair.


**Decision.** A function and a kind are built by a chain, the commands written on their noun: `::fn | takes … | impl …`. The literal folds the chain into a quote beside the recipe, `::fn[~(takes :by |~~ How much. ~~| /0) ~(add by)]`, and a call and a constructor read the roles of the chain. A payload that is no pair of a quote and a recipe is refused as `…/PayloadNotPairError`, so `::kind[[] null]` is refused so. A step of the chain that its noun does not take, a command unknown or one written with args it does not take, `returns #[]` or `takes :true`, is refused as `::fn/CommandNotTakenError` and `::kind/CommandNotTakenError`, whose page names the args each command takes. Where those args are spelled is D272's.


**Why.** One name for one thing: the page of `::fn` calls the folded quote of the commands a signature, and the first page, [QSBLD] and its section `## chain` call it the chain. The critic's counterexample: no word for the chain alone cures a name that says “not commands” of commands written right, while `CommandNotTakenError` is exact for every case today, whatever D272 decides, and joins the NotTaken family of the refusals of what a holder takes.


**Cost.** `::fn/SignatureNotCommandsError` and `::kind/SignatureNotCommandsError` become `…/CommandNotTakenError`, with their pages, laws and cases (QSBLD/fn, QSBLD/kind, QSBLD/answer). The probe `::kind[[] null] !| type` answers `::kind/PayloadNotPairError`. [QSBLD#fn], [QSBLD#answer] (an empty set) and [QSBLD#kind] are written anew. The pages of `::fn` and `::kind` (“the commands of its signature”, “A step of a recipe is no command of a signature”, “A signature is a quote”). In the code, `readSignature`, `signatureOf`, `EMPTY_SIGNATURE` and `readKindSignature` → the chain (`core/src/runtime/signature.mjs` and its callers). The sister project renames the refusal where it reads it.


**Removes.** The refusal tags `::fn/SignatureNotCommandsError` and `::kind/SignatureNotCommandsError`, renamed and narrowed; flagged, reason one name for one thing. No capability leaves.


**The maintainer's words.** none on chain or signature

#### 6. A host's implementation and the host

**Road.** Recipe is the slot, `::builtin{}` its descriptor and primitive the host's function; implementation, kernel and the host's bind leave. Host keeps the core as a host of its own sources, the critic's conflict answered; the engine is the platform.


**Decision.** A verb's recipe is set by `impl`, its input `:recipe`: code, or `::builtin{}`, the descriptor of a recipe a host answers by a primitive. A primitive is the JavaScript function a host hands for that address beside its source, `{ uri, source, primitives }`, which the evaluator runs over the values of the inputs. The evaluator reads its table of primitives and the kinds `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`. The root refuses a `::builtin{}` its host handed nothing for as `::builtin/PrimitiveNotHandedError`, a primitive handed for a name declared otherwise as `::builtin/PrimitiveNamesNoBuiltinError`, and a primitive handed twice for one address as `::builtin/AddressHandedTwiceError`. To bind is to declare a name. A host is a tool that embeds the language: the command line, the site, the sister project, and the core, a host of its own sources whose verbs answer as every host's do [QSNAM#owner]. The engine a host runs on is its platform, as `:longest` of `range` and the regexes every platform reads alike, and the terminal is what hands a paste in chunks.


**Why.** One name for one thing. One model: the core is a host of its own sources [QSHST#source], so its verbs take no second name, and the road that named them the runtime's is set aside. `::builtin` is the maintainer's word and stays. Kernel is the word he found strange. Bind is the language's word for a declaration, `::bind` and `::binding`, so the host's act of handing a primitive takes the chapter's own verb, hand.


**Cost.** The host interface's key `impls` → `primitives` (`core/README.md`, `cli/src/cli-sources.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/root.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/index.mjs`, `core/src/session.mjs`); the sister project renames it in its sources. The three `::builtin/…` defects are renamed, with the case `an-invariant-of-the-kernel-sits-under-builtin` (QSERR/site) and D340 part 12's text. In the code, `hostImplOf`, `HOST_IMPLS`, `bindPrim` and `bindStateReader` → primitive and hand (232 uses of `bindPrim` and kin in `core/src`, mechanical). [QSHST#source] (kernel, implementation, binds) and [QSHST#repl] (a paste a host hands). The head of `core/lib/qlang/builtin.qlang`, the head of `core/src/runtime/root.mjs`, `core/lib/qlang/number.qlang` (`:longest`), `core/lib/qlang/regex.qlang` and `cli/src/line-editor.mjs`. The aim's “A primitive enters”, which means a verb, says verb.


**Removes.** The refusal tags `::builtin/ImplementationNotHandedError`, `::builtin/ImplementationNamesNoBuiltinError` and `::builtin/AddressBoundTwiceError`, and the interface key `impls`, all renamed; flagged, reason one name for one thing. `::builtin{}` stays. No capability leaves.

#### 8. Shape, and outline

**Road.** Shape is one thing, the structure of a value, which a template writes and `shape` reads; its other senses are repaired to their own words, and schema and outline leave.


**Decision.** A shape is the structure of a value, beneath its tags: its keys, its nesting and the kinds of its parts. A template of `get` writes the shape of the answer it builds, `get {:who /first :next ~(get /age | add 1)}`. `shape` reads the shape of a value as its literal under `::shape`: `[1] | shape` answers `::shape[::number]`. The kind beneath a stack of tags is a kind of the core. The keys of a map travel with its values. The evaluator reads the kinds `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`. The parser refuses a form. A value comes apart into its atoms and the template that builds it back. `paths` answers the leaves of a value, each the pair of the path that reads it and its kind.


**Why.** One name for one thing, and words carry paradigms. The benchmark asks each answer in the habitual sense, “Expected shape: a JSON array of strings.”, which is what `shape` answers, so the structure a template writes and the schema `shape` reads are one concept, while the value beneath the tags, the keys of a map and the forms of the grammar are other things. The maintainer chose `::shape` for the verb's answer as the higher-entropy domain, and his question whether the verb's shape and a literal's are two things gets its answer: one structure, read and written.


**Cost.** The first page (“then the shape beneath them” → the kind of the core beneath them). `core/lib/qlang/map.qlang` (“its keys are the shape that travels with them”) and [QSCOL#map]. [QSLIT#atoms] (“a shape” → a template). [QSHST#source] (“the shapes it reads”). [QSPTH#words] (“the outline of a value”, “the schema of a value”). The comments of `core/src/grammar.peggy` (“in the shape of `location()`”, “block shape”). `shapeKindOf` in `core/src/ordering.mjs` → the kind of the core. The aim's “the outline of a value” and “its outline”, and D327's text, which follow at its decision. No case, word or refusal of the surface changes.


**Removes.** none

#### 9. Env and scope

**Road.** Scope is the one word for the names a step reads, in the texts, the code and the interface of the package; `env` stays the verb alone.


**Decision.** A scope is the names a step reads. The steps of one pipeline, its first step and every step `|` or `!|` joins to it, run in one scope; a group, each run of a quote and each call of a verb run in a scope of their own; and the root lies under them all. `env` answers the declarations of the reader in the scope where the step stands: `:x 1 | env | keys` answers `#[:x]`. The code and the interface of the package name the scope `scope`: `state.scope`, `createSession({ scope })`, `session.scope`, `scopeAfterCell`, and the root scope `langRuntime` answers.


**Why.** [QSWRK#code] and one name for one thing. The aim's core concept is “a state pair of a value and a scope”, and the first page says “The scope of a step is what the steps before it wrote”, while the code names the same map env and scope by turns (`recipeScopeOf`, `nodesAndLabelsOf` answering `{ scope }`). The verb `env` keeps the habit of `env`, the names a user set.


**Cost.** 377 uses of `env` and kin in 17 files of `core/src` (`state.mjs`, `env-keys.mjs` → `scope-keys.mjs`, `eval.mjs`, `session.mjs`, the runtime), 36 in the hosts, `core/README.md` (“`env` is the current scope”) and [QSNAM#scope] (“run in one env”). The sister project renames `createSession({ env })`, `session.env` and `envAfterCell`. `process.env` is Node's and stays.


**Removes.** The interface names `env`, `session.env` and `envAfterCell`, renamed; flagged, reason one name for one thing. The verb `env` stays. No capability leaves.


**The maintainer's words.** «странно это смотрится | env» (maintainer, 2026-09-25 15:37 UTC, session 86982eb5), on the verb, which he later ran as `env | keys` (2026-10-08 19:12, 22b934f8); none on the name of the scope.

#### 10. Frame

**Road.** The text as it stands: frame names one thing, a level of the stack of calls, now that the scope over the root is no frame in the tree (fa876c69, D338's work).


**Decision.** A frame is one level of the stack of calls. Each step of a call runs a frame below it against the budget of depth, refused past it as `::call/EvaluationDepthExceededError` with its `:depth` and `:limit`, and the trail of a failure holds a frame for each level it left, `::frame[subject steps place]`.


**Why.** One name for one thing already holds. The critic read it so: one paradigm, the depth and the path of a failure counted in one kind of level. The maintainer liked `::frame`.

#### 11. Source, uri and origin

**Road.** `:source` keeps its one sense, what was written; where a text came from is its origin in every fact of the language; the host's interface keeps `uri`, the word of the formats a host meets.


**Decision.** The source of a binding is the step that declared it, the source of a failure to parse is the line where the reading stopped, and the source of a host is the text it hands. The axis `source` reads every declaration written under an address. Where a text came from is its origin, a keyword: `:origin` of a binding, `:qlang/vec` or `:cell-1`, and of a failure to parse, `"add (" | parse !| get /origin` answering `:string/parse`. A host hands each source with its `uri`, which the core records as the origin.


**Why.** One name for one thing, one fact one spelling: the fact of where a text came from is spelled `:origin` (a keyword) on a binding and `:uri` (a string) on a parse failure. Words carry paradigms: `:uri` wakes a URI, which `string/parse` is not. Borrow concepts, not formats: `uri` stays where the language meets a host, as the language server's documents write it.


**Cost.** The fact `:uri` of `::quote/ParseError` becomes `:origin` (its page `::quote/ParseError/uri`, `core/src/error-convert.mjs`, `core/src/parse.mjs`), with [QSSYN#refusal] and [QSQUO#parse] (“the `:uri` of `parse`”) and their cases. The root's defects name the origin.


**Removes.** The fact name `:uri` of a failure to parse, renamed; flagged, reason one fact one spelling. No capability leaves.


**The maintainer's words.** none

#### 12. Parse, both ways

**Road.** A road of the model's own: parse reads text alone, and the text of a quote's code is its `toString`, as every atom's text is; the involution of `parse | parse` leaves.


**Decision.** `parse` reads text: `::string/parse` reads a text into the quote it writes, and a blank source into the empty quote. `parseJson` reads JSON into a value. `toString` writes the text a value stands for: a keyword its name, a number its digits, and a quote its code, `~(add 1 | mul 2) | toString` answering `"add 1 | mul 2"`, which `parse` reads back as the quote. A link or a snippet answers the query it holds. `print` writes the literal, `~(add 1)` with its brackets, and `json` writes its JSON.


**Why.** One name for one thing. Words carry paradigms: parse wakes reading text into structure, never writing it, while the text of code is the habit of `toString`. And one road: `toString` refuses a quote today with three near names, `:nearest [:json :parse :print]`, which is the main road the maintainer asked for being missing. Of the involutions themselves the maintainer said they are an amusing effect and no more.


**Cost.** `::quote/parse` leaves and `::quote/toString` takes its page and its five laws. The texts that write a quote as text by `parse`: the law `:errors/holdsTheSkippedSteps` of the first page, the laws of `source` in `any.qlang`, `core/lib/qlang/quote.qlang`, and the cases of QSERR/trail, QSERR/site and QSQUO/parse (about 25 places). `bindPrim('quote/parse', …)` in `core/src/runtime/code.mjs`. [QSQUO#parse] is written anew. The sister project writes `toString` where it wrote a quote's `parse`.


**Removes.** The verb `::quote/parse`, removed, its capability answered by `::quote/toString`; flagged as a removal of a verb, reason in the aim: one name for one thing, words carry paradigms, one road.

#### 13. The refusal of what a holder takes is named two ways

**Road.** The two refusals of a subject stay apart, each with its sentence, since residence and kinds are two rules with two sets of facts; for the records of the steps, the record's road: a payload of another kind is refused by the role of the holder, with `:expected`.


**Decision.** A value of none of the kinds a holder takes is refused under the holder, with the kinds as `:expected`. An input, a field and the answer are refused as `…/KindNotTakenError`, an element as `…/ElementKindNotTakenError`, and a payload as `…/PayloadKindNotTakenError`, the records of the steps `::call`, `::bind` and `::construct` among them: `::call[1] !| get /expected` answers `#[::map]`. A map whose fields break the schema of its record is refused as `…/PayloadNotSchemaError`, with the `:field` it names. The subject of a verb is served by residence and refused under the verb by its label, `…/SubjectNot<Label>Error`, with no `:expected`. The subject of a function `apply` runs is held to the kinds `on` names and refused as `::fn/call/SubjectKindNotTakenError`, with them as `:expected` [QSERR#site].


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: a payload of the wrong kind is refused one way on every kind, `::fail`/`::group`, `::number` and `::doc` alike. The critic's counterexample: the residence refusal and the kinds refusal carry other facts, and [QSERR#site] keeps them apart. The maintainer chose the name by the role with the kinds as `:expected`.


**Cost.** New refusals `::call/PayloadKindNotTakenError`, `::bind/PayloadKindNotTakenError` and `::construct/PayloadKindNotTakenError`; `PayloadNotSchemaError` narrows to a map. `recordConstructor` in `core/src/runtime/quote-steps.mjs` and the pages `call.qlang`, `bind.qlang` and `construct.qlang`. [QSQUO#step] is written anew, and the probe `::call[1] !| get [~(type) /field]` answers `[::call/PayloadKindNotTakenError null]`.


**Removes.** none


**The maintainer's words.** «что ошибки часть публичного апи .. а такие сборные на лету имена очень хрупки при развитии ..» (maintainer, 2026-10-14 22:53 UTC, session d65e4c1d) and «мне тоже второй вариант» (23:01), the name by the role with the kinds as `:expected` (D287).

#### 14. Place, index and key

**Road.** Index for the number of a position, key for an entry of a map, segment for either written in a path, place for where a part is read from; the fact that holds the segment written in an index's stead is `:index`.


**Decision.** An index is the number of an element's position in a vector or a set, from zero, `/-1` the last. It is `:index` of `::path/IndexOutOfBoundsError` and of `…/ElementKindNotTakenError`, and of `::path/IndexNotIntegerError`, which holds the segment written where an index stands: `[10 20] | get /name !| get /index` answers `:name`. A key names an entry of a map. A segment of a path is a key or an index, which `segments` answers, `/a/0 | segments` answering `[:a 0]`. A place is where a part is read from: the place of an input in the args, a path or code over them, and the place a frame names, the path of the part a verb's code broke on, `/1`.


**Why.** One name for one thing. Words carry paradigms: index is the habit of a position, as the facts already say. Place then means one thing, a read of a part, in an input as in a frame, which part 1's `.index` of the code follows.


**Cost.** The fact `:key` of `::path/IndexNotIntegerError` becomes `:index`, with its page and cases (QSPTH). The prose that says place for a position, in [QSPTH#get], [QSPTH#words], [QSCOL] and [QSBLD#input], the pages of path, vec and set, and the comments of `core/src/path-read.mjs` and `core/src/types.mjs` (`makePath`: “a place of a vector”).


**Removes.** The fact name `:key` of `IndexNotIntegerError`, renamed; flagged, reason one name for one thing. No capability leaves.


**The maintainer's words.** none

#### 15. Marker, mark, absent, each and address

**Road.** The elision is the marker, and the caret of a parse failure takes its own name. Mark stays the tag of a shape, absent the key some records lack, and null the kind of null. The section `## each` takes the chapter's word image, and the address of an email becomes an email.


**Decision.** An elision, a value of `::elision`, is the marker of what a view left out; its `:read` continues the query to the part. A failure to parse marks where the reading stopped by its `:caret`. A mark is a tag of a shape: `::absent` over a key some records lack, and `::nullable` over a part null in some. `::null` is the kind of null. The kind `::each` folds a map in a shape. The section of [QSCOL] where `map` runs its code on each element is `## image`. The keys of a map that read as data are ids, dates, times, uuids and emails.


**Why.** One name for one thing. The maintainer reads the elision as a marker, so the caret takes the word that diagnostics give it. `::absent` is his. A section anchor named like a kind of the shape leads a reader of `[QSCOL#each]` to the wrong page, and the chapter already calls what `map` answers per element its images.


**Cost.** The fact `:marker` of `::quote/ParseError` becomes `:caret` (`core/src/error-convert.mjs`, `core/src/parse.mjs`, `::quote/ParseError/marker`, [QSSYN#refusal], [QSQUO#parse] and their cases), unless D339 part 6 takes the fact away. `core/lib/qlang/null.qlang` (“the absent value”). `core/test/conformance/QSCOL/each.jsonl` → `image.jsonl`, with the 13 citations of `[QSCOL#each]`. `core/lib/qlang/each.qlang` and `core/src/runtime/path.mjs` (“an address”).


**Removes.** The fact name `:marker` of a failure to parse, renamed; flagged, reason one name for one thing. No capability leaves.


**The maintainer's words.** «я думаю элайд это прост специальный маркер..» (maintainer, 2026-10-15 22:05 UTC, session 70702c43); «ок, и с ::absent согласен - хороший сигнал для моделек..» (2026-10-15 16:46, 70702c43).

#### 16. Anchor and name, right and left

**Road.** `:anchor` is the input of every axis that takes a name after its subject. `and` and `or` take the `:other` boolean the algebra's way, and the subject is never the left.


**Decision.** An axis given a name after its subject takes it as its `:anchor`, a keyword, the last name of a node under the subject: `doc`, `binding`, `source` and `runLaws` alike, so `::vec/take | source 1 !| type` answers `::any/source/anchor/KindNotTakenError`. `and` and `or` join the subject with the `:other` boolean, as `union`, `minus` and `inter` join the subject with the `:other` set. The comparisons compare the subject with a `:value`.


**Why.** One name for one thing. The first page calls the keyword after `doc` an anchor, the word of hypertext. The infix habit of right and left has no place in a pipeline whose first operand is the subject. Which axes take an anchor stays D294's, so the critic's conflict does not arise.


**Cost.** `::any/source/name/KindNotTakenError` → `::any/source/anchor/KindNotTakenError`, and `::boolean/and/right/KindNotTakenError` and `::boolean/or/right/KindNotTakenError` → `…/other/…`, with their pages, laws and cases (QSSLF/axis, QSCND/boolean). The page of `source` and the pages of `and` and `or` (“the left”, “the right”).


**Removes.** The input names `:name` of `source` and `:right` of `and` and `or`, renamed with their refusal addresses; flagged, reason one name for one thing. No capability leaves.

#### 17. The kinds of the steps

**Road.** Each step and quote is named by the thing it is: `::catch` for the step that catches, `::invariant` beside `::law` for the quotes the chain writes. `::bind`, `construct/construct` and the field `:payload` stay, each with its sentence.


**Decision.** A declaration is the step `::bind`, which writes a `::binding`. A step of the fail track, which catches, is held as `::catch`: `~(add 1 !| 0) | query /1` answers `::catch~(0)`. The step of a tagged literal is `::construct{:tag … :payload …}`, its payload the literal's; `::construct/construct` builds it, as every kind's constructor builds its values. The quote of an invariant stands under `::invariant`, written by `holds`, as the quote of a law stands under `::law`, written by `proves`: `~(:x 1 | ::Box[1] !| 0) | map ~type` answers `[::bind ::construct ::catch]`.


**Why.** One name for one thing. Words carry paradigms: `::fail` reads as a failure, while the step recovers one. The prose, the code (`INVARIANT_TAG`) and the page of `::holds` all say invariant, so the tags name the thing (`::invariant`, `::law`) and the commands of the chain the act (`holds`, `proves`). The maintainer's binding steps keep `::bind`.


**Cost.** The tags `::fail` → `::catch` (`core/lib/qlang/fail.qlang` → `catch.qlang`, `FAIL_TAG`, `::fail/PayloadKindNotTakenError`, [QSQUO#step], the first page's errors chapter and its link) and `::holds` → `::invariant` (`core/lib/qlang/holds.qlang` → `invariant.qlang`, `INVARIANT_TAG`, the first page's verbs chapter, [QSBLD#label] and [QSBLD#chain], `:Positive ::holds~(gt 0)`), with their cases. The sister project renames the tags where it reads them.


**Removes.** The tags `::fail` and `::holds`, renamed; flagged, reason one name for one thing and words carry paradigms. No capability leaves.


**The maintainer's words.** «это больше как такой базовый holds..» (maintainer, 2026-10-13 19:26 UTC, session d65e4c1d), on the command; «когда у инварианта есть имя - это можно использовать» (2026-10-09 11:29, 22b934f8); «фактически все биндинг шаги» (2026-10-08 00:23, 22b934f8).

#### 18. A refusal named for a rule that left

**Road.** A road of the model's own, between renaming and the critic's: the key is refused under the verb by its role, with the kinds a key files under as `:expected`; the composite name of `minus` and `inter` joins it.


**Decision.** A key of `groupBy`, `indexBy` and `toMap` files under the keyword `toKeyword` answers for it, which holds for a string, a number, a boolean, null, a tag and a keyword. A key of another kind is refused under the verb as `…/KeyKindNotTakenError`, with its `:index` and, as `:expected`, the kinds a key files under, spelled once in the code beside `keywordFiledFor`: `[{:k [1]}] | indexBy /k !| type` answers `::vec/indexBy/KeyKindNotTakenError`. The keys `minus` and `inter` take off a map are keywords or strings, and another key is refused as `…/KeyKindNotTakenError` with `:expected #[::keyword ::string]`.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling. The name says what fails: the key files under no keyword, while a key need not be one. This joins the role-and-kinds rule the maintainer chose, and takes away a composite name of the kind he called brittle. A refusal under the call of `toKeyword` would name no site the reader wrote, so the critic's road is set aside.


**Cost.** Six refusal addresses: `vec`/`set` `groupBy`/`indexBy`, `vec/toMap`, and `map`/`vec` `minus`/`inter`. Their pages and their cases (QSCOL/group, QSCOL/algebra, QSCND/convert). `core/src/runtime/vec.mjs`, `convert.mjs` and `setops.mjs`.

#### 22. The path of a failure

**Road.** Trail is the word of what a failure flew, and path names a value of `::path` alone.


**Decision.** The trail of a failure is the vector of the frames it left, a value of `::trail` under `:trail`, written as the failure flies. A path is a value of `::path`, and the fact `:path` is the path a read followed. An error at rest whose `:trail` holds no frame starts its trail over when it is raised again.


**Why.** One name for one thing. The word is the layer of the first trail (a8cb6c67), which the frames kept. The maintainer asked for the trail to be a tag of its own.


**Cost.** The pages of `::error`, `::trail` and `::frame` (“the path a failure flew”), and `core/lib/qlang/map.qlang`; [QSERR#trail] and [QSERR#print]; the comments of `core/src/eval-trail.mjs`, `core/src/runtime/raise.mjs` and `core/src/types.mjs`; and the case names `an-error-raised-again-resumes-its-path-at-its-last-frame` and `a-tagged-error-literal-whose-trail-is-no-path-is-refused-when-raised` (QSERR/trail).

**Road.** Regex everywhere, the kind's own name; pattern leaves.

A string that writes no regex is refused as `::string/toRegex/TextNotRegexError`: `get [~(#"a" | type) ~("(" | toRegex !| type)]` answers `[::regex ::string/toRegex/TextNotRegexError]`.


**Why.** The maintainer's principle: regex has one exact sense, while pattern names many things, a design pattern among them, which is how the maintainer uses it. One name for one thing. The kind, its verb and the code already say regex, and the maintainer calls the thing регексп.

The sister project renames the input and the refusal where it reads them.

#### 24. Body and recipe, and the scope of the code a verb runs stated twice

**Road.** The record's road: recipe is the code a verb runs, body what a declaration binds, and the scope a call opens is stated once, at [QSBLD#recipe], after the inputs it holds are defined.


**Decision.** The recipe of a verb is the code it runs, code or `::builtin{}`, which `impl` sets. A recipe runs in the scope its call opens: the root, the args as `args`, each input by its name, the nodes and the labels of the scope it is called in, and the names its steps declare, `args` and an input none of them. [QSNAM#scope] names that scope among the scopes. The body of a declaration is the step after its name, the field `:body` of a `::bind`, which runs once against the value in the pipe.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling: two lists of one scope. The order of concepts is the order of dependencies: the scope holds the args and the inputs, which [QSBLD#input] defines. One name for one thing: the body of `:number/f (::fn | …)` is the function, not the code it runs.


**Cost.** [QSNAM#scope] (“The body of a word runs in the scope its call opens”), [QSNAM#word], [QSNAM#owner], [QSQUO#held] (“The scope of a word's body”) and [QSBLD#recipe] are written anew; `evalBody`, `bodyEnv` and `CALL_BINDS_KEY` of `core/src/eval.mjs` and `core/src/env-keys.mjs`. No case changes.

#### 25. Expected, two things under one fact, and the rules of the parser among the facts

**Road.** The fact keeps its name on both refusals, each declaring its own, since expected is the right habit for each; the parser's names for its rules give way to the words of the language. Splitting the fact is set aside.


**Decision.** `:expected` of a refusal of kinds is the set of the kinds the holder takes, `#[::number]`. `:expected` of a failure to parse is the continuations the reading could take there, beside what it met as `:found`. Each fact is declared under its own refusal, `::error/expected` and `::quote/ParseError/expected`, with its page and its kind. A continuation is the text of a literal, or the keyword of what a rule of the grammar reads, in the words of the language: the letter or `_` that begins a name, a letter of a name, whitespace, a doc, a character of the text. The walk of the editor finds the occurrences of a name.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms (“a word stays where the habit it wakes is the right one”): expected/actual and expected/found are each the habit of its reader, and the mission's first attempt is more likely with both. The one spelling of each fact is its declaration under its refusal. The parser's “identifier” and “char-class” name in a fact a reader reads what the language calls a name and its letters [QSNAM#letters].


**Cost.** The descriptions of `IdentStart` and `IdentTail` in `core/src/grammar.peggy`, and the class continuation of `liftExpectedAlternative` in `core/src/error-convert.mjs`, change the continuations the cases of [QSSYN#refusal] expect: `":" | parse !| get /expected` answers `["\"" :"a letter or _ that begins a name"]` or whatever the wording ends up being. `findIdentifierOccurrences` (`core/src/walk.mjs`) and its caller in the language server.

**Road.** The record's road: two facts, the repair of a call under `:repair`, the language's word for what a refusal teaches, and `:read` of an elision as the first page teaches it.


**Decision.** A refusal that names the step a reader likely meant, where a path stands for the value it names, holds that step as `:repair`: `/tags | count !| get /repair` answers `~(get /tags | count)`, and `5 | mul /qty !| get /repair` answers `~(mul (get /qty))`. An elision holds as `:read` the query that, continued from the answer, reads the part left out: `100 | range | elide 10 | last | get /read` answers `~(drop 12)`.


**Why.** One name for one thing: one replaces the step on its subject, the other continues from the answer, and a reader who learned the first page's “whose `:read` continues the query to it” would run the refusal's on the wrong value. Repair is the word of the pages and of `explain`, “the page saying how to repair the call”.

**Road.** One road taught, `parse | open`, the road whose meaning needs no explanation; `apply` stays the verb that runs code on a subject.

#### 1. What a call takes: modifier, object, argument, args, part, input, selector and params

**Road.** D276's third road, with the maintainer's word for the value: modifier is the code written after a verb's name, args are the values that code computes, input is what a function declares; the verb `inputs` answers the inputs and `params` stays beside it as the habit's name; object, argument and part leave.


**Decision.** A command is a verb and its modifiers. A modifier is the code written after the verb's name in a step, which the call computes once against the subject of the step. `::call{:name … :modifiers […]}` holds the modifiers as written: `~(add x) | first | get /modifiers` answers `[::call{:name :x}]`. The values the modifiers compute are the args of the call, and one of them is an arg. A recipe reads them as `args`, a value under the tag of the verb's address: `:number/a (::fn | takes :xs |~~ X. ~~| ~(it) | impl ~(args)) | 5 | a (add 1) ~(add 1)` answers `::number/a[6 ~(add 1)]`. `apply` hands a function its args as a vector, `5 | apply f [3]`, and `fill` writes them into the function's code. `reduce` calls its verb with the value so far as the subject and the element as its arg. A function declares its inputs by `takes`, each with its name, its page, the place of the args it reads (`/0` or `~(query /0 1)`) and its kinds, and a value of none of the kinds is refused under the input's name. `inputs` answers them, each a map of its name, page, place and kinds. `params` stands beside it as the name a reader's habit reaches for: a recipe that calls `inputs`, its page naming `inputs` [QSWRK#catalog]. Object names nothing a call takes; it stays JSON's word for a map. Argument names no call's args, only what a shell hands a process. Part names no arg: the flags of `toRegex` are its input `flags`, “the flags are an input of `toRegex`, `toRegex "i"`”. The value a query starts from is its subject, “A query starts from a subject, the value it is given”, and the command line's standard input keeps its name [QSHST#input].


**Why.** Words carry paradigms (aim). The tree already parts the written step from its value, `:modifiers` from `args`, and [QSBLD#input] states it: “the args of a call, the values its modifiers computed against the subject”. Args is the maintainer's word for the O of SVO. He chose it over an object that mixes with OOP's objects and with JSON's object, which is the form of the mission's data. One fact, one spelling: what a function declares is answered by a verb named with the language's own term, and the habit's name is kept as a second road to it [QSWRK#catalog]. So a reader who reads “its inputs” on the page of `::fn` finds `inputs` at the first attempt, and a reader of habit finds `params`. The critic's hole is closed: there are three words for three things, and `:modifiers` names the step written.


**Cost.** No refusal, fact or case answer changes. Added: `::fn/inputs` with its page and laws; `::fn/params` becomes a recipe that calls it. Rewritten pages: (a) `::fn` in core/lib/qlang/fn.qlang: its head, `:fn/takes`, `:fn/fill`, `:fn/call`, and the laws that say object or arguments (`:answersTheFailureOfAnObject`, `:refusesAStepWrittenForItsQuote`, `:call/refusesArgumentsPastItsInputs`, `:SignatureNotCommandsError/refusesACommandWithNoObjects`, `:SignatureNotCommandsError/refusesAnObjectPastTheKinds`); (b) `::kind`; (c) `apply` in core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, with `:runsAFunctionOnItsArguments` and `:args/NotTakenError/refusesTheArgumentsOfAQuote`. In the first page: its first sentence, the sentence of `reduce`, which calls the element the modifier, and “each of its objects” in the chapter of code. Elsewhere: the repair of `toRegex` in `REPAIRS` of core/src/pattern.mjs and its law `:TextNotPatternError/namesTheFlagsAsAPart`; the head of cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang; site/src/pages/index.astro; core/README.md (“the parts of its args”). Sections written anew at a move: [QSNUM#arithmetic], [QSNUM#combine], [QSCOL#reduce], [QSSLF#catalog], [QSBLD#fn], [QSBLD#input] (“a key of an object”) and [QSQUO#held]. In the code, `objectsOf` (core/src/runtime/signature.mjs) and `selectorOfPlace` (core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs) take the words args and place [QSWRK#code], as [D338] items. A host loses nothing: `params`, `args` and every address stay.


**Removes.** None. `inputs` is added and `params` stays, as the habit's road to it.

#### 2. The role of a tag that names what lies under it: label, noun, kind, tag and topic

**Road.** Keep the defined terms, repair each use to its term, and write the two definitions of a label as one.


**Decision.** A label is a name declared with a kind, either by a chain from `::kind` or by its page alone; a label declared by its page alone is a kind of no commands. Its verbs are functions named under it, its nodes lie under its address, `kinds` lists it, and its tag lays over a payload as it is: `:Box |~~ B ~~| | ::Box | get [~(binding | get /value | type) ~(kinds | has ::Box) ~(::Box[1] | type)]` answers `[::doc true ::Box]`. A noun is the head of an address, the name its path names before its last name. It is declared before the names under it, else `::bind/NounNotDeclaredError`. A noun may be a label (`::Box` of `::Box/area`), a verb (`::number/add` of `::number/add/addend`) or a name that holds data (`x` of `:x/y`). A kind is what the tag of a value names and what `type` answers. A tag is a kind's name written as a value, `::Box`. A topic is a label whose page tells the language or a host, `::qlang` and `::cli`, and what `tells` declares under it is a chapter, `::qlang/values`. [QSBLD#label] states a label once; [QSNAM#address] and [QSNAM#reach] cite it, the latter saying “A label, a topic among them, is reached by its tag”. No text uses one of these five words for another.


**Why.** Words carry paradigms, and the maintainer named two of these himself, label (ярлык) and noun (существительное), each with one sense the tree keeps. Folding label, noun and topic into kind and tag would leave the noun that is a verb or a name of data with no term (the critic's counterexample). The two definitions of a label become one once a label of a page alone is the kind of no commands the first page already calls it. One fact, one spelling.


**Cost.** No refusal, case, fact or word of the surface changes. Sentences change: the head of QSBLD (“a label is a kind”), [QSBLD#label], [QSNAM#address], [QSNAM#reach] and [QSSLF#page] (“the concepts the first page names”), at a move. The page of `kinds` in core/lib/qlang/any.qlang says chapter, not concept: “A chapter of the topic is no kind”, and its law `:holdsNoConcept` is named for the chapter. The page of `::kind` and the first page's chapter of verbs change one sentence each.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «добавь в наш словарь терминов "легенда", "легенда полезной нагрузки" "ярлык"» (maintainer, 2026-10-08 23:30, session 22b934f8); «одно я знаю что надо держаться за существительные» (2026-10-07 17:29, 22b934f8).
