#### 3. Verb and word

**Road.** Verb everywhere: the frame of subject, verb and object the maintainer named, with the first page's chapter and the refusals following it. Word is left to a word of text.


**Decision.** A verb is a function named under a label. A step calls it by its name, on a subject that the walk of its tags brings to the label, and it lives at its address, `::vec/sort`. The chapters, the pages, the first page and the code all say verb: the first page says “A verb is a function named under a label”, the chapter QSDSP is titled “A verb found through its subject”, and the section [QSNAM#word] becomes [QSNAM#verb]. Word names a word of text alone, a run of text that whitespace sets off: a modifier of more than one word stands in parentheses, `true`, `false` and `null` are the words of the literals (“`:true` is the word of a literal”), and `contains` finds a word of a string. A value named by a verb's name meets the verb aloud: `:count 5 | [1 2] | count !| type` answers `::call/ValueMeetsVerbError`, beside `::call/KindAnswersNoVerbError`. `verbNames`, `verbsReaching` and the chapter `::qlang/verbs` keep their names, and the package exports `isVerb` and `declaresVerb`.


**Why.** This follows the maintainer's frame, subject, verb and object, and his word at this sitting: high-entropy words are preferred. Verb names one thing in the texts. Word names four: a function under a label, a run of text, the word of a literal and a word of a string, which is the critic's counterexample to word everywhere. Verb was the first page's word until the chain of `::fn` brought word in (7e7bf3b0). The surface is stable this way: the refusal by kinds, the first page's chapter, the exports three hosts read, the laws' keys and the benchmark's tasks (“the pages of the verbs”) already say verb. So this road renames one refusal, where word everywhere would rename all of those.


**Cost.** Surface: `::call/ValueMeetsWordError` becomes `::call/ValueMeetsVerbError`. That moves its page and laws in core/lib/qlang/call.qlang, its case lines in [QSCOL#reduce] and [QSNAM#reach], the unit tests that name it, and the open records D304 and D326 that cite it. The exports `isWord` and `declaresWord` of core/src/index.mjs, read by lsp/src/features.mjs and cli/test/error-tag-catalog-drift.test.mjs, are renamed. Laws whose names say word are renamed, `:isAWordNamedUnderALabel` and its kin (`git grep -h -o -E "proves :[A-Za-z/]*Word[A-Za-z/]*" -- core/lib cli/lib`, keyword aside). Case names that say word for a verb are renamed, each under a record in transit over its section. Texts: every chapter and page where word names a function under a label, including the first page's chapters 1 and 4 and its chapter of verbs; the title and file name of the chapter QSDSP and the links to it; the section [QSNAM#word] and its citations; “The words of strings” at the head of [QSTXT]. Code names are [D338] items: `isWord`, `applyWord`, `nearestWords`, and `declaresWordIn` of the language server. The painter's kind `'word'` of `tokenize` follows whatever vocabulary [D340] part 3 decides. A host whose catalog pages say word, or whose tests catch the refusal, renames in one release; for the sister project this is said, not measured.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «по той же причине и глаголы перестали быть сами по себе .. потому что их мало, а существительных много .. существительное в начале сильнее снимает энтропию» (maintainer, 2026-10-08 00:44, session 22b934f8); «::Box/put[10]  итого это у нас раскладывается на  ::Box - S (subject), put - V (verb), а vec [10] - это O (object)» (2026-10-09 03:33, 22b934f8); «высокоэнтропийные слова у нас предпочтительнее же» (2026-10-16 12:17, d7f52e95). #### 4. Member and node

**Road.** Node for what lies under a noun; member leaves; the code names the value of a verb by the verb; the parser's node stays inside the code.


**Decision.** A node is a binding of the tree of names: what lies under a noun at an address, as `::vec/sort` lies under `::vec`. `manifest` lists the nodes, and an anchor reaches one under its subject, `::vec | doc :sort`. The axes read the node the subject holds under the anchor, the nodes of the topic are its chapters, and the names near a miss are the nodes near it. Member leaves the texts and the code. In the code, the value of a verb, a function or a quote under a label, is named by the verb. The parser's tree keeps its nodes inside the code, and a page names one only as “a node of the parser's tree”. `:node` stays a platform of `:host`, the value of the Node loader.


**Why.** The maintainer cautioned against the abstractions of objects, and member is a word of OOP; the sister project's domain names the members of Java classes with it. Node is already the first page's word (“declares a node under the noun”) and the package's (`nodeOf`, `nodeAddresses`), so the larger surface keeps its word. The critic's hole is answered: the parser's nodes and Node's platform are outside the language's texts.


**Cost.** No refusal or fact changes. In core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, the pages of the axes `doc`, `binding`, `source`, `runLaws`, `laws` and `tutorial` (“the member the subject holds under it”) are rewritten, and so are their laws `:readsAMemberByAnAnchor`, `:UnknownNameError/refusesAnAnchorNoMemberAnswers` and `:UnknownNameError/namesTheMembersNear`. Sections [QSSLF#axis], [QSSLF#near] and [QSSLF#page] are written anew at a move, and any case name that says member is renamed under its record. Code names are [D338] items: `memberRecordOf` (core/src/runtime/axis.mjs), `nearestMembers` (core/src/nearest-names.mjs), `isMemberValue` (core/src/runtime/nouns.mjs) and `callMemberOn` (core/src/eval.mjs, read by core/src/runtime/print.mjs). D294's road then reads “the anchor of a node”.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «там где-то в доках было про что стоит подальше держаться от ООП и т.п. объектовых абстракций..» (maintainer, 2026-10-08 21:27, session 22b934f8); «у java.lang.String вызывали и получати под 200 мемберов» (2026-09-26 06:10, 86982eb5), the members of the sister project's domain.

#### 5. Chain, signature and commands

**Road.** A road of my own: chain and signature are kept, each for its own thing, and the page of `::kind` says signature as the page of `::fn` does; the two refusals keep their names, and the args of a command are left to D272.


**Decision.** A chain is the commands written from the noun, each a verb of `::fn` or `::kind` whose subject is the value built so far, with the recipe last: `::fn | takes … | impl ~(…)`. A signature is the commands a function or a kind holds, a quote, the first element of its literal: `::fn[signature recipe]` and `::kind[signature check]`. A call and a constructor read the signature. A command is a step that calls a verb, including a step of a chain. The page of `::kind` calls the first element of its literal its signature, as the page of `::fn` does. The head of QSBLD says that a verb and a label are each built by a chain, whose signature a call and a constructor read. `::fn/SignatureNotCommandsError` and `::kind/SignatureNotCommandsError` keep their names: each refuses a signature that is no quote of commands, each command with the args its verb takes. Which part of the tree reads those args is [D272]'s question.


**Why.** The maintainer's words split the signature, the contract, from its implementation, which is exactly the pair `::fn[signature recipe]`. Signature carries the paradigm of a declared interface, a function's signature or an ML signature. Chain names the pipeline written from the noun, the recipe included, so they are two things and not one under two words. The critic's counterexample, a command written right with an arg of the wrong kind, concerns the second half of the refusal's name; that half is honest once D272 settles who reads each command's args, so renaming the first half would cure nothing. Stability: two addresses and their laws stay.


**Cost.** No refusal, case or word changes. One sentence each: the page of `::kind` in core/lib/qlang/kind.qlang (`SignatureNotCommandsError`: “The commands of a kind are a quote” becomes “The signature of a kind is a quote”) and the head of QSBLD. [QSBLD#fn] is checked at a move.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «может надо сигнатуру (контракт) описывать отдельно, а содержимое реализации отдельно» (maintainer, 2026-09-25 17:32, session 86982eb5); «как получить наивысшую эмержентность в этой теме с описанием сигнатур и глаголов..» (2026-09-25 19:43, 86982eb5).

#### 6. A host's implementation and the host

**Road.** A road of my own: one word for each thing, recipe for the slot, `::builtin{}` for the descriptor, primitive for the host's function, register for handing it to the table, kernel for the evaluator with its table, and host for whatever hands the language its sources, the core included. `impl`, `impls` and `::builtin` stay.


**Decision.** The recipe is what a function runs or a kind checks with: a quote, or `::builtin{}`. `impl` gives it. `::builtin{}` is the recipe of a verb or a kind whose primitive the host hands. A primitive is the JavaScript function a host hands for an address whose recipe is `::builtin{}`, in the `impls` of its source, `{ uri, source, impls }`. The root refuses a `::builtin{}` for which no primitive was handed as `::builtin/PrimitiveNotHandedError`, and a primitive handed for any other name as `::builtin/PrimitiveNamesNoBuiltinError`. A module of the runtime registers its primitives under their addresses, and two under one address are refused as `::builtin/AddressRegisteredTwiceError`. The kernel is the evaluator with its table of primitives and the values it reads, `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`. A host is a program that hands the language sources and their primitives and receives its answers: the command line, the language server, the site, a tool that embeds the language, and the core, the host of the language's own sources [QSHST#source]. A failure of any host's primitive is `::call/HostFailureError`. The engine a host runs on is the engine, and the terminal a host reads from is the terminal. The painters read the verbs of the root, which `verbNames` answers.


**Why.** Primitive has one sense in the texts. Implementation has two: the command `impl` takes a quote as the implementation too. Bind and binding name a declaration, `::bind` and `::binding`, so registering a primitive takes its own word. The critic's conflict is resolved inside one model: the core remains a host of its own sources and is not renamed the runtime's, which keeps “a word written in the language and a word of the host answer alike” [QSNAM#owner]. A host keeps `impl`, `impls` and `::builtin`, the maintainer's descriptor. Host means one thing, so the engine and the terminal stop borrowing it.


**Cost.** Refusals: `::builtin/ImplementationNotHandedError` becomes `::builtin/PrimitiveNotHandedError`, `::builtin/ImplementationNamesNoBuiltinError` becomes `::builtin/PrimitiveNamesNoBuiltinError`, and `::builtin/AddressBoundTwiceError` becomes `::builtin/AddressRegisteredTwiceError`. These are defects of the boot that no query reaches, held by core/test/unit/primitives.test.mjs and session.test.mjs and no case. A host's test that names one breaks; for the sister project this is said, not measured. Pages: `::builtin` (core/lib/qlang/builtin.qlang), `:fn/impl`, `:kind/impl`, regex.qlang (“every host reads alike” becomes engine) and number.qlang (“a vector of the host” becomes engine). Sections [QSHST#source] and [QSHST#repl] (“A paste a host hands over” becomes terminal) change at a move, along with the comment in cli/src/line-editor.mjs. The option `builtinNames` of `tokenize`, with cli/src/render.mjs, cli/src/repl.mjs, cli/src/highlight-ansi.mjs and lsp/src/features.mjs, follows whatever vocabulary [D340] part 3 decides. Code names are [D338] items: `HOST_IMPLS`, `hostImplOf`, `declareHostValue`, `bindPrim` and `bindStateReader`.


**Removes.** None.

#### 8. Shape, and outline

**Road.** A road of my own: shape takes one sense, the structure of a value, written as the `::shape` the verb answers; outline and schema leave; every other sense of shape takes its own word.


**Decision.** The shape of a value is its structure, the kind at each of its parts. The verb `shape` answers it as a value of `::shape`: `[1] | shape` answers `::shape[::number]`. A template of `get` builds an answer of a shape, and a task asks for an answer of a shape, “Expected shape: a JSON array of strings”. The shape of a literal is what `shape` answers for it. `paths` answers the leaves of a value, each the pair of its path and its kind. The first page says that `shape` answers the kind at each part of the subject, with no schema, and the aim says shape where it said outline. Every other sense takes its own word: the walk of a subject's tags ends at the value beneath them; a map's keys travel with its values; the kernel reads values, `::fn`, `::kind` and `::builtin{}`; a value comes apart into its atoms and a template that builds it back [QSLIT#atoms]; and the code's `shapeKindOf`, together with “the shape of a container” in its comments, names the kind of the core.


**Why.** This answers the maintainer's question whether the shape a verb answers and the shape of a concrete literal differ: they do not. It keeps his words `shape` and `::shape`, and the habit of the benchmark's tasks, which the critic found asking in the same sense. One word, one exact sense: outline named two different answers, the `::shape` of `shape` and the leaves of `paths`, and schema brings in the format of JSON Schema, which the aim's principle (borrow concepts, not formats) keeps out.


**Cost.** No refusal, case, verb or kind changes. First page: “`shape` its schema” and “then the shape beneath them” change. Pages rewritten where they say outline or a second sense of shape: `paths` in core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, `::shape`, `::each`, `::absent`, `::nullable`, and the head of `::map`. Sections [QSPTH#words], [QSLIT#atoms] and [QSHST#source] (“the shapes it reads”) are written anew at a move. Also changed: docs/journal/AIM.md (the mission and the principle “Looking into an answer is safe”), and the text of [D327], which then speaks of shape. Code comments are [D338] items: core/src/ordering.mjs (`shapeKindOf`), core/src/types.mjs and core/src/runtime/path.mjs.

#### 9. Env and scope

**Road.** Scope is the language's word for the names in force; `env` is the verb for the reader's part of them; the code names a scope as the language does, and the session's API keeps its key.


**Decision.** The scope of a step is the names in force there, over the root: what the steps before it in its pipeline declared, and the scope around a group or a quote. A group, each run of a quote and the call of a verb open scopes of their own. The verb `env` answers the reader's part of the scope, what the reader declared, the root apart: `:x 1 | env | keys` answers `#[:x]`. The code names a scope a scope [QSWRK#code]. A session holds its scope as `env`, in `createSession({ env })` and `session.env`, which core/README.md calls the scope the session holds. `langRuntime` answers the scope of the root. The command line's `process.env` is the environment of its process.


**Why.** Lexical scope is the habit, and the chapters already say scope for the names in force. One word, one exact sense: [QSNAM#scope] spoke of “one env” in the language's own text, and README called `env` the current scope. `env` is the verb the maintainer typed, and it matches the shell's habit, `env` listing the variables a session set. Stability: hosts write `createSession({ env })`.


**Cost.** No refusal, case or verb changes. Changed: the sentence of [QSNAM#scope] at a move, core/README.md (“`env` is the current scope”), and the comments of core/src/state.mjs and core/src/runtime/index.mjs (“The environment every query starts from”). Code names, renamed per [QSWRK#code] as [D338] items: `state.env`, `envSet`, `withEnv`, `envHas`, `rootOf` and core/src/env-keys.mjs, about three hundred lines across the core (`git grep -c -E '\benv\b|envSet|withEnv|envHas' -- core/src`). The Tier 2 path of env-keys.mjs in CLAUDE.md changes with that rename.


**Removes.** None.

#### 10. Frame

**Road.** The text as it stands: frame has one sense, a level of the stack of calls. The frame over the root is renamed as an item of D338.


**Decision.** A frame is one level of the stack of calls a query runs. The budget of depth counts the frames nested under the query, and a verb that recurses without end is refused on the frame past the limit, `::call/EvaluationDepthExceededError`. The trail holds one frame for each level a failure left, a value of `::frame`. The run of a source of the root is a cell, as a cell of a session is, and the comment of core/src/runtime/root.mjs and the names of nouns.mjs call it a cell [D338].


**Why.** One paradigm, the stack frame: the depth of a failure and its path are counted in the same kind of level, as the critic's road showed. The one other sense, the frame over the root, moves no rule.

#### 11. Source, uri and origin

**Road.** Rename so that `:origin` is the one fact of where a text came from; `:source` is kept in its one sense, the source as written.


**Decision.** `:origin`, a keyword, names where a text came from. It stands on a binding, where `::vec/count | binding | get /origin` answers `:qlang/vec`, and on a failure to parse and on the refusals of the root, where `"add (" | parse !| get /origin` answers `:string/parse`. The fact `:uri` leaves the errors. A host names its source `uri` in `{ uri, source, impls }`, and `:origin` is the keyword of that name. `:source` is the source as written: the text a host hands, the quote of the step that declared a binding, the line where a parse stopped, and what the axis `source` reads under an address.


**Why.** One fact, one spelling (aim): the origin of a text had two names and two kinds, a keyword on a binding and a string on a failure. The four uses of `:source` are one notion in three forms, text, quote and line, so they keep their word.


**Cost.** Changes the shape of a failure to parse and of three refusals of the root: `::quote/ParseError/uri` (core/lib/qlang/quote.qlang) and the `:uri` of `::builtin/BootstrapSourceMissingError`, `::builtin/RootSourceNotDeclaredError` and `::builtin/PrimitiveNamesNoBuiltinError` become `:origin`, a keyword. Code: `errorFromParse` in core/src/error-convert.mjs, and core/src/session.mjs. A host that reads `uri` off a failure reads `origin`.

#### 12. Parse, both ways

**Road.** A road of my own: `toString` writes a quote's text, as it writes a keyword's name, and `parse` reads text alone. The word `parse` on a quote leaves.


**Decision.** `parse` reads text into the quote it writes: `"add 1 | mul 2" | parse` answers `~(add 1 | mul 2)`. `toString` writes the text of a quote without its brackets: `~(add 1 | mul 2) | toString` answers `"add 1 | mul 2"`, just as `toString` writes a keyword and a tag name by their names. `parse` reads that text back as the quote. `print` writes the quote's literal, `"~(add 1)"`, and `json` its JSON, while `parseJson` reads JSON into a value. A link or a snippet gives the text of its query by `toString`, and the empty quote gives the empty string. A source read by the axis `source` is written as text by `toString`.


**Why.** The maintainer's words: holding the involution is «просто забавный эффект и не более»; models suffer when the language offers no one direct road; and conversions follow the convention «toString toMap toVec toNumber toTag». [QSWRK#catalog] says a name that answers otherwise than the habit expects fails in silence: `parse` wakes the habit of reading text, and on a quote it wrote. `toString` wakes the right habit, the source text of code as JavaScript's `Function.prototype.toString` gives it, and on a quote it now answers what its refusal today sends the reader to look for (its `:nearest` is `[:json :parse :print]`).


**Cost.** `::quote/parse` becomes `::quote/toString`, with its page and laws (core/lib/qlang/quote.qlang, including the involution law `parse | parse`, which becomes `toString | parse`), and the primitive in core/src/runtime/code.mjs. Every use of `| parse` on a quote changes: the first page's `:errors/holdsTheSkippedSteps`, `source | parse` in core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, and the cases of [QSQUO#parse] (core/test/conformance/QSQUO/parse.jsonl) and of `source | parse` (QSERR/site.jsonl, QSNAM/address.jsonl, QSSLF/axis.jsonl, QSDSP/refusal.jsonl). The probe of [QSHST#source] changes. [QSQUO#parse] and [QSCND#convert] (“a keyword and a tag name by their names, a quote by its code”) are written anew at a move. A host query that writes a quote's text by `parse` changes in one release; for the sister project this is said, not measured.


**Removes.** Removal, flagged: the word `parse` on a quote, `::quote/parse`. Its capability, writing the text of a quote, stays as `::quote/toString`.

#### 13. The refusal of what a holder takes is named two ways

**Road.** The record's road for the records of the steps: a payload that is no map is refused by the holder's kinds. The two rules of [QSERR#site] stay two.


**Decision.** A holder refuses a value of none of the kinds it takes as `…/KindNotTakenError` or its kin, with `:expected`. The holders are an input, an answer, a payload, an element, and the subject of a function `apply` runs, `::fn/call/SubjectKindNotTakenError`. A verb refuses a subject its walk does not bring to its label as `…/SubjectNot<Label>Error`, which names its label and holds no `:expected` [QSERR#site]. The records of a step take a map, as every kind takes its payload. `::call`, `::bind` and `::construct` refuse a payload of another kind as `…/PayloadKindNotTakenError` with `:expected #[::map]`, as `::fail` and `::group` refuse theirs. `…/PayloadNotSchemaError` refuses a map whose field breaks the record, `:field` naming the field.


**Why.** One model: a payload of another kind is refused by the same rule in every kind, and `:field null` with its page's “no Map”, a word of the JavaScript, leaves. The critic's counterexample holds and is kept: refusal by kinds and refusal by residence are two refusals with facts of their own, and folding them would rewrite [QSERR#site]. The maintainer asks of a refusal a unique name for the signal, which both rules keep.


**Cost.** Changes the refusal a payload that is no map meets under `::call`, `::bind` and `::construct`: `::call[1] !| type` moves from `::call/PayloadNotSchemaError` to `::call/PayloadKindNotTakenError`, carrying `:expected`. Code: `recordConstructor` in core/src/runtime/quote-steps.mjs. Pages: `::call`, `::bind` and `::construct` each gain the refusal. [QSQUO#step] is written anew at a move, with a case under it. No case has met the old refusal for this payload; only the record's probe did.

#### 14. Place, index and key

**Road.** Keep each word, with the sentence that defines it, and repair the prose to the definitions.


**Decision.** An index is the position of an element of a vector, or of a set in its one order: a whole number from zero, with `/-1` counted from the end, as `:index`, `::path/IndexOutOfBoundsError` and `::path/IndexNotIntegerError` hold. `IndexNotIntegerError` holds as `:key` the key a path read where an index stands: `[10 20] | get /name !| get /key` answers `:name`. A key is the name of an entry of a map. A place is a path to a part: what an input reads of the args, `/0` or a quote that reads them; what a field reads of a payload; and the path of the part a frame names, which `place` answers. Prose that says place for the position of an element says index.


**Why.** Stability, since the gain of a rename is small: the facts and refusals already say index and key, and `:key` on `IndexNotIntegerError` is honest under these definitions. The drift lives in the prose (“A vector is read by a place”), which then says one word for each thing.


**Cost.** No refusal, fact, case or verb changes. Prose changes: the page of `::path` and of `::path/IndexNotIntegerError` with its laws (“A vector is read by a place, not a field”), the page of `::error/index`, the sentences of set.qlang (“`:index` naming its place in the one order”), desc.qlang and vec.qlang, and [QSPTH#value] and [QSLIT#atoms] at a move.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** None found on this axis.

#### 15. Marker, mark, absent, each and address

**Road.** Keep each tag's word: an elision is a value of `::elision`; marker is left to the caret; the tags of a shape are tags; absent is left to `::absent`; `::each` and the section [QSCOL#each] both stay; an email is an email.


**Decision.** An elision is a value of `::elision`, standing for a part a view left out: `:size` counts what it stands for and `:read` is the read that reaches it. Marker names the caret that a failure to parse draws under its place, `:marker`, whose fate [D339] part 6 holds. `::absent`, `::nullable` and `::each` are tags of a shape: a key some records lack stands under `::absent`, a part null in some of the data stands under `::nullable`, and a map keyed by data stands under `::each`. Mark leaves the texts as a noun. `::null` is the kind of null, the value that stands for none; absent names only the key a record lacks. `::each` is the kind of a fold of a shape, and [QSCOL#each] is the section of the specification that tells how `map` runs over each element. An address is the address of a name, `::vec/sort`; a key of data that reads as an email is an email.


**Why.** A value of a kind takes its tag's word, as a value of `::frame` is a frame. Marker and mark were near words over different things, and the caret keeps marker because its fact is named so. The maintainer accepted `::absent` and `::each`. The section anchor [QSCOL#each] is the specification's and no reader of the language meets it, so it stays.


**Cost.** No case, refusal, fact or tag changes. Prose changes: the pages of `::elision` and `elide` (core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, “markers under `::elision`”, “the marker of a text”); the chapters of errors and hosts on the first page; cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang; the pages of `::absent`, `::nullable`, `::each`, `::shape` and `::null` (“the kind of the absent value”); and the comment of core/src/runtime/path.mjs (“an address”). Sections [QSPRN#print], [QSPRN#elide], [QSHST#answer], [QSPTH#words] and [QSSLF#page] (“meets a marker”) change at a move. [D327]'s text then speaks of elisions.

#### 16. Anchor and name, right and left

**Road.** `anchor` becomes the one input of the axes for the last name; `and` and `or` take a `condition`, named for its role as the arithmetic's inputs are; the comparisons' `value` and the algebra's `other` stay.


**Decision.** An axis that takes the last name read under its subject takes it as its input `anchor`: `source` does, as `binding`, `doc` and `runLaws` do, and `::vec/take | source 1` is refused as `::any/source/anchor/KindNotTakenError`. `and` and `or` take a `condition`, the boolean joined to the subject, as `if` takes one: `true | and 1` is refused as `::boolean/and/condition/KindNotTakenError`. Their pages name the subject and the condition, with no left and no right. The comparisons take a `value` and the algebra an `other`, each named for its role, as `add` takes an `addend` and `div` a `divisor`.


**Why.** One role, one word: the last name read is an anchor, the hypertext's word, on every axis. Positional names belong to infix operators, which qlang has none of. Condition is the language's own word for a boolean a verb holds (“A condition is a boolean”), with one exact sense. Which axes take an anchor at all is still [D294]'s question.


**Cost.** Three refusal addresses change: `::any/source/name/KindNotTakenError` becomes `…/anchor/KindNotTakenError`, and `::boolean/and/right/KindNotTakenError` and `::boolean/or/right/KindNotTakenError` become `…/condition/KindNotTakenError`. Changed with them: their laws in core/lib/qlang/any.qlang and core/lib/qlang/boolean.qlang, the cases that name them, and [QSSLF#axis] and [QSCND#boolean] at a move. A query positions its args, so none breaks; a test that catches the old address does.


**Removes.** None.

#### 17. The kinds of the steps

**Road.** Keep each tag, with the sentence that defines it.


**Decision.** The records of the steps keep their tags, and each page names the step it records in the words of the texts. `::bind` is the step of a declaration, `:x 1`. `::call` is the step of a command. `::construct` is the step of a tagged literal, `::Box[1]`, and its field `:payload` holds the payload the literal writes. `::group` is the step of a group. `::fail` is the step written after `!|`, on the fail track. The node of an invariant holds its condition under `::holds`, written by the command `holds`, and the node of a law holds its query under `::law`, written by `proves`.


**Why.** Stability: code as data reads and writes these tags, `::bind{…}` and `::fail[…]`, and the gain of renaming them is small. Bind is the maintainer's word for the steps that declare, and holds is his word for an invariant.


**Cost.** No tag, refusal or case changes. One sentence each on the pages of `::bind`, `::construct`, `::fail`, `::holds` and `::law`, and [QSQUO#step] at a move.

#### 18. A refusal named for a rule that left

**Road.** A road of my own: the key is held to the kinds that file under a keyword, by the refusal of kinds the language already has, in the reader's terms rather than `toKeyword`'s call refusal.


**Decision.** `groupBy`, `indexBy` and `toMap` file each key under a keyword by `toKeyword`'s rule [QSCND#convert]: a keyword as itself, a string or a number under the keyword of its text, a boolean or null under that of its literal, and a tag name under that of its name. A key of another kind (a regex, a path, a vector, a set, a map or a quote) is refused as `…/KeyKindNotTakenError`, with `:expected #[::boolean ::keyword ::null ::number ::string ::tag]` and the `:index` of its element: `[{:k [1]}] | indexBy /k !| type` answers `::vec/indexBy/KeyKindNotTakenError`.


**Why.** `KeyNotKeywordError` names a rule that left, since a string files under a keyword. The record's road, refusing as `toKeyword` refuses, would answer `::call/KindAnswersNoVerbError` named `:toKeyword` to a reader who called `groupBy`. That is not in the reader's terms. The refusal of kinds with `:expected` [QSERR#site] says the rule in one spelling, and `toKeyword`'s rule stays the one that files.


**Cost.** Five addresses change: `::vec/groupBy/KeyNotKeywordError`, `::vec/indexBy/KeyNotKeywordError`, `::vec/toMap/KeyNotKeywordError`, `::set/groupBy/KeyNotKeywordError` and `::set/indexBy/KeyNotKeywordError` become `…/KeyKindNotTakenError`, and their facts gain `:expected`. Changed with them: their pages in core/lib/qlang/vec.qlang and core/lib/qlang/set.qlang (which then name the regex and the path among the refused kinds), core/src/runtime/vec.mjs and core/src/runtime/convert.mjs, and the cases of [QSCOL#group] and [QSCND#convert] at a move.

#### 25. Expected, two things under one fact, and the rules of the parser among the facts

**Road.** A road of my own: `:expected` is kept on both refusals, each declared under its own label as it is today, the habit of every parser; the grammar's rules are described in the words of the language.


**Decision.** `:expected` holds what could have stood where a value or a text was refused. For a holder, it is the kinds the holder takes, a set of tags, `::error/expected`. For a failure to parse, it is what the reading could take there, a vector, `::quote/ParseError/expected`, beside `:found`. The rules of the grammar that a failure lists are described in the words of the language, in core/src/grammar.peggy and core/src/error-convert.mjs: the letter a name begins with and a letter of a name [QSNAM#letters], whitespace, a doc, and a class of characters by what it reads, the end and any character. The walk that finds the occurrences of a name calls them names.


**Why.** Expected and found are the pair every parser's refusal wakes, and the facts are already two, one under each label, so splitting them would cost the habit for little gain. The descriptions of peggy's rules, `:"identifier start (letter or underscore)"` and `:char-class`, are words of the implementation that reach the reader's facts, where the language speaks of a name and its letters.


**Cost.** No fact or refusal is renamed. Changed: the display names of `IdentStart`, `IdentTail` and the classes in core/src/grammar.peggy (parser rebuilt); `liftExpectedAlternatives` and `classKeyword` in core/src/error-convert.mjs; and the cases of [QSSYN#refusal] that read those descriptions (core/test/conformance/QSSYN/refusal.jsonl), at a move. #### 29. An opened error and an error at rest

**Road.** Rename to one word for the state, at rest, with catch for the act; opened leaves.


**Decision.** `!|` catches a failure, its step running on the error at rest. An error that a catch made a value is at rest, and one raised again resumes its trail. The texts, [QSERR#raise], [QSERR#trail], [QSSLF#explain] and the laws say at rest: `:raisesAnErrorAtRestAsItself`, `:asksAFailureAtRest`, `:readsAnErrorAtRest` and, on the first page, `:catchesAFailureToItsFacts`. Opened leaves them, and `open` stays the verb that runs held code.


**Why.** There were two words for one state. At rest is the section's word and the code's (`errorAtRest`), and catch is the habit of try and catch, which the first page already uses. This also frees open for the verb `open`.


**Cost.** Laws renamed: core/lib/qlang.qlang (`:opensAFailureToItsFacts`), core/lib/qlang/any.qlang (`:readsAnErrorOnceOpened`, `:raisesAnOpenedErrorAsItself`, `:asksAnOpenedFailure`) and core/lib/qlang/map.qlang (`:raisesAnOpenedErrorAsItself`). Prose: core/lib/qlang/error.qlang, map.qlang and any.qlang. [QSERR#raise], [QSERR#trail], [QSSLF#explain] and [QSPTH#words] (“a set opened as a vector” becomes “read as a vector”) change at a move. No refusal or case answer changes.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** None found on this axis.

#### 30. Tuple

**Road.** Keep tuple for the shape's vector of one length whose places part; the answer of a template is named for the template.


**Decision.** A tuple is a vector of one length in a shape whose places part, `[A B]`, read place by place: `:readsTuplesPlaceByPlace`, `tupleOf`, [QSPTH#words]. The answer of a template of paths is the vector or the map the template builds. The laws `:readsATuple` and `:readsATupleSoftly`, and the cases `get-reads-a-tuple` and `query-reads-a-tuple-softly`, are named for the template.


**Why.** Tuple wakes the habit of a tuple type, a fixed length with a kind at each place, which is exactly the shape's sense. A template's answer is the literal it is written as.


**Cost.** Two laws in core/lib/qlang/any.qlang, and two case names in core/test/conformance/QSPTH/template.jsonl under a record in transit over [QSPTH#template]. No answer changes.


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** None found on this axis.

#### 31. Wrapper

**Road.** Rename in the code to the language's words: a value under a tag, and the records of the steps by their tags.


**Decision.** The code names a value under a tag as the language does, a payload held beneath its tag with the class `underTag`. It names the records `::fail` and `::group` by their tags, as the records of a step that hold one step. Wrapper leaves the comments and the names of core/src/types.mjs (`makeTagged`, the tags of the steps) and core/src/runtime/quote-steps.mjs (`wrapperConstructor`).


**Why.** [QSWRK#code]: the code names each thing as the language names it. Wrapper named two things and was a third name beside `underTag`.


**Cost.** Code comments and names only, as items of [D338]. No rule changes.


**Removes.** None.

#### 32. Plain, over three things

**Road.** Keep plain for a value under no tag; a primitive that reads no state takes the values alone; JavaScript's objects are called so.


**Decision.** Plain names a value under no tag: a plain map, a plain vector. A primitive that reads no state of its call takes the values alone, and the heads of the runtime's modules, the head of cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang and [QSHST#source] say so (“every other primitive takes the values alone”). An object of JavaScript is called an object of JavaScript.


**Why.** Plain in the language's sense matches the habit of a plain object, one with nothing over it. The other two uses were the implementation's words in the texts.


**Cost.** No rule changes. Changed: [QSHST#source] at a move, the head of cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang, and the comments of core/src/runtime/vec.mjs, arith.mjs, control.mjs, setops.mjs and tagged.mjs and of core/src/parse.mjs, as items of [D338].


**Removes.** None.

#### 33. A print read back two ways

**Road.** The record's road choosing `parse | apply it`, the road the first page, the pages and the cases already teach; `open` is kept for a link and a snippet.


**Decision.** A print reads back by `parse | apply it`: `{:a 1} | print | parse | apply it` answers `{:a 1}`, and a text that writes its own head runs the same way. `parse` reads the text into a quote and `apply` runs it, its subject the quote itself, which a print never reads. `open` runs the query of a link or a snippet from null, beneath its tags. The page of `::cli`, cli/README.md and the page of `open` read a text back by `parse | apply it`, as every other text does.


**Why.** Stability, since the gain is small: the two roads agree on every print, and `parse | apply it` stands in the first page's laws, the pages, the chapters and the cases (QSQUO/apply.jsonl among them), where `parse | open` stands in five lines. One road is taught, and open keeps the one sense the hypertext gives it.


**Cost.** No rule, case or answer changes. Changed: cli/lib/qlang/cli.qlang (“`in | parse | open` reading it back”), cli/README.md, and the page of `::quote/open` in core/lib/qlang/quote.qlang (its prose, its input's page and its law `~("1 | add 1" | parse | open | eq 2)`, which shows a link's query instead).


**Removes.** None.


**The maintainer's words.** «а open это же просто  apply payload или как?» (maintainer, 2026-09-28 10:47, session d427ce7e), a question and no choice.
